# Editorial Preface

This omnibus volume joins the full chapter sequence into a single dossier-length publication. It preserves the chapter-level evidence apparatus so the reader can move continuously through the argument while still inspecting the bounded evidence surface for each chapter.

## How to Use This Volume

- Read each chapter as a standalone paper if needed; chapter-local appendices remain attached.
- Use the corpus companion for the full-source, full-claim, full-entity, full-event, and full-note audit surfaces.
- Treat `Documented Fact`, `Disputed Fact`, `Hypothesis / Interpretation`, and `Speculative Narrative Risk` as distinct evidentiary states throughout the volume.

## Shared Citation and Evidence Rules

Body notes point to the real external documents first, then to the chapter-local appendix entries that explain why those sources matter in context. Chapter bibliographies remain local so each chapter still works when extracted from the omnibus.

# Chapter 00. Opening framework

## Abstract

The fight over artificial intelligence is not only a fight over products, model benchmarks, or corporate market share. By December 17, 2024, the Bipartisan House Task Force on Artificial Intelligence had already recorded the training-data conflict in terms overlapping with the dossier's own framework: reproduction, derivative-work theory, commercialization, consent, credit, and compensation. The dossier therefore does not end in a vague call for better ethics.

## Keywords

Opening framework; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism; AI weights; patrimony of humanity; evidence-based dossier

## Research Question

How should the dossier's opening framework connect surveillance capitalism, ownership of AI weights, and the three-reform program?

## Scope and Framing Note

This paper examines opening framework within the broader dossier *From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism*. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper that frames the civilizational ownership question and announces the three-reform destination. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 01, Chapter 04, Chapter 10, but those cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic comprehension.

## Central Thesis

The fight over artificial intelligence is not only a fight over products, model benchmarks, or corporate market share. It is also a fight over who owns the condensed value of collective human cognition. In this dossier, the central question is whether captured human language, art, code, labor, culture, science, and emotion can be turned into privately governed strategic weights without triggering a deeper political challenge. ^[Sources: TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-00-appendix-a1-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law)); TwinChat, [How AI Is Ruining Your Life to Build...](https://medium.com/twinchat/how-ai-is-ruining-your-life-to-build-an-empire-8b6d02e55bc9) (n.d.; see [Appendix A2](#ch-00-appendix-a2-how-ai-is-ruining-your-life-to-build-an-empire))]

This chapter also sets the repo's two writing layers. The files in `chapters/` are the reference-corpus narrative: readable, source-traceable, and explicitly bounded. The files in `papers/` are the durable paper-format outputs: self-contained, numerically cited, linked to a global appendix, and reusable for later publication or adaptation. The two layers serve different audiences, but they defend the same argument. ^[Sources: U.S. House of Representatives, [Bipartisan House Task Force Report on...](https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf) (2024-12-17; see [Appendix A3](#ch-00-appendix-a3-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence)); TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-00-appendix-a1-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law))]

## Evidence and Method Note

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 00 and is grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

## Introduction

This paper asks: How should the dossier's opening framework connect surveillance capitalism, ownership of AI weights, and the three-reform program? Its working answer is clear from the start: The fight over artificial intelligence is not only a fight over products, model benchmarks, or corporate market share. It is also a fight over who owns the condensed value of collective human cognition. In this dossier, the central question is whether captured human language, art, code, labor, culture, science, and emotion can be turned into privately governed strategic weights without triggering a deeper political challenge. ^[Sources: TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-00-appendix-a1-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law)); TwinChat, [How AI Is Ruining Your Life to Build...](https://medium.com/twinchat/how-ai-is-ruining-your-life-to-build-an-empire-8b6d02e55bc9) (n.d.; see [Appendix A2](#ch-00-appendix-a2-how-ai-is-ruining-your-life-to-build-an-empire))]

This chapter also sets the repo's two writing layers. The files in `chapters/` are the reference-corpus narrative: readable, source-traceable, and explicitly bounded. The files in `papers/` are the durable paper-format outputs: self-contained, numerically cited, linked to a global appendix, and reusable for later publication or adaptation. The two layers serve different audiences, but they defend the same argument. ^[Sources: U.S. House of Representatives, [Bipartisan House Task Force Report on...](https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf) (2024-12-17; see [Appendix A3](#ch-00-appendix-a3-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence)); TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-00-appendix-a1-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law))] The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

## Historical or Institutional Context

By December 17, 2024, the Bipartisan House Task Force on Artificial Intelligence had already recorded the training-data conflict in terms overlapping with the dossier's own framework: reproduction, derivative-work theory, commercialization, consent, credit, and compensation. Footnote `40` on physical page `116` cites Mark Uriostegui's essay directly. That does not prove the argument on the merits, but it does prove institutional uptake. ^[Sources: U.S. House of Representatives, [Bipartisan House Task Force Report on...](https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf) (2024-12-17; see [Appendix A3](#ch-00-appendix-a3-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence)); TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-00-appendix-a1-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law))]

## Main Analysis

Paul Lara's published writing adds the dossier's second framing line: technofeudalism, debt-heavy buildout, paper valuation, and the smoke-economy vocabulary needed to describe how extraction is financed and politically sold. The dossier uses that line as interpretive framing, not as a substitute for primary evidence. ^[Sources: Paul Lara, [¿Cómo hackear al tecnofeudalismo?](https://www.dineroenimagen.com/paul-lara/como-hackear-al-tecnofeudalismo/186955) (2026-06-29; see [Appendix A4](#ch-00-appendix-a4-c-mo-hackear-al-tecnofeudalismo)); Paul Lara, [La gran deuda por la IA](https://www.dineroenimagen.com/paul-lara/la-gran-deuda-por-la-ia/186930) (2026-06-22; see [Appendix A5](#ch-00-appendix-a5-la-gran-deuda-por-la-ia)); TwinChat, [How AI Is Ruining Your Life to Build...](https://medium.com/twinchat/how-ai-is-ruining-your-life-to-build-an-empire-8b6d02e55bc9) (n.d.; see [Appendix A2](#ch-00-appendix-a2-how-ai-is-ruining-your-life-to-build-an-empire))]

Interpretation: the strongest defensible reading is not that every later chapter proves a single grand conspiracy. It is that a coherent line can be drawn from platform extraction, to social weakening, to pandemic acceleration, to model training, to labor correction, to access chokepoints, and finally to legitimacy and capture. The burden of the dossier is to keep the factual layer and the political interpretation distinct while still arguing forcefully from the evidence. ^[Sources: TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-00-appendix-a1-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law)); TwinChat, [How AI Is Ruining Your Life to Build...](https://medium.com/twinchat/how-ai-is-ruining-your-life-to-build-an-empire-8b6d02e55bc9) (n.d.; see [Appendix A2](#ch-00-appendix-a2-how-ai-is-ruining-your-life-to-build-an-empire)); U.S. House of Representatives, [Bipartisan House Task Force Report on...](https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf) (2024-12-17; see [Appendix A3](#ch-00-appendix-a3-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence))]

## Position Within the Series

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest companions are Chapter 01 (Social networks as infrastructure for free data capture), Chapter 04 (AI arrives when the human raw material already exists), Chapter 10 (What to do). In that series logic, Chapter 00 frames the civilizational ownership question and announces the three-reform destination.

## Counterarguments

The opening can overreach if it treats the enclosure argument as already complete in legal doctrine rather than politically and institutionally emergent. The House citation proves relevance, not victory. The Lara framing sharpens the language of the dossier, but it does not replace the need for documentary support chapter by chapter. ^[Sources: U.S. House of Representatives, [Bipartisan House Task Force Report on...](https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf) (2024-12-17; see [Appendix A3](#ch-00-appendix-a3-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence)); Paul Lara, [La gran deuda por la IA](https://www.dineroenimagen.com/paul-lara/la-gran-deuda-por-la-ia/186930) (2026-06-22; see [Appendix A5](#ch-00-appendix-a5-la-gran-deuda-por-la-ia))]

## Limits of the Evidence

The opening can overreach if it treats the enclosure argument as already complete in legal doctrine rather than politically and institutionally emergent. The House citation proves relevance, not victory. The Lara framing sharpens the language of the dossier, but it does not replace the need for documentary support chapter by chapter. ^[Sources: U.S. House of Representatives, [Bipartisan House Task Force Report on...](https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf) (2024-12-17; see [Appendix A3](#ch-00-appendix-a3-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence)); Paul Lara, [La gran deuda por la IA](https://www.dineroenimagen.com/paul-lara/la-gran-deuda-por-la-ia/186930) (2026-06-22; see [Appendix A5](#ch-00-appendix-a5-la-gran-deuda-por-la-ia))]

## Reform Relevance

The dossier therefore does not end in a vague call for better ethics. It moves toward three explicit reforms:

- `Refactor Section 230`
- `AI Weights as Patrimony of Humanity`
- `Limit Government Capture by AI Companies`

Interpretation: those reforms are not presented as already enacted or legally settled. They are the dossier's normative answer to the documented structure of extraction, enclosure, and capture. ^[Sources: TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-00-appendix-a1-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law)); TwinChat, [How AI Is Ruining Your Life to Build...](https://medium.com/twinchat/how-ai-is-ruining-your-life-to-build-an-empire-8b6d02e55bc9) (n.d.; see [Appendix A2](#ch-00-appendix-a2-how-ai-is-ruining-your-life-to-build-an-empire)); U.S. House of Representatives, [Bipartisan House Task Force Report on...](https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf) (2024-12-17; see [Appendix A3](#ch-00-appendix-a3-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence))]

## Conclusion

The dossier therefore does not end in a vague call for better ethics. To test that argument, the dossier has to begin before frontier AI. In the architecture of this series, Chapter 00 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

## Notes

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among `Documented Fact`, `Disputed Fact`, `Hypothesis / Interpretation`, and `Speculative Narrative Risk`. # Chapter 01. Social networks as infrastructure for free data capture

## Abstract

Before AI could be marketed as a new intelligence order, social platforms had already built the behavioral raw material of that order. The FTC's 2024 report on social media and video-streaming data practices gives this chapter its strongest regulatory anchor. Once social life is reorganized around measurable engagement, extractive targeting, and recommendation systems, platforms stop looking like passive infrastructure and start looking like privately governed civic systems.

## Keywords

Social networks as infrastructure for free data capture; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism; Section 230 reform; evidence-based dossier

## Research Question

How does social networks as infrastructure for free data capture function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish?

## Scope and Framing Note

This paper examines social networks as infrastructure for free data capture within the broader dossier *From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism*. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper that builds the structural case for refactoring Section 230 by documenting recommendation, extraction, and design power. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 02, Chapter 04, Chapter 10, but those cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic comprehension.

## Central Thesis

Before AI could be marketed as a new intelligence order, social platforms had already built the behavioral raw material of that order. Their core innovation was not simply communication at scale. It was the normalization of a business model in which attention, relationships, preferences, and expression became persistent, monetizable data assets. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-01-appendix-a1-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); FTC, [FTC Imposes $5 Billion Penalty and Sw...](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2019/07/ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-facebook) (2019-07-24; see [Appendix A2](#ch-01-appendix-a2-ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-and-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-on-facebook))]

## Evidence and Method Note

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 01 and is grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

## Introduction

This paper asks: How does social networks as infrastructure for free data capture function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer is clear from the start: Before AI could be marketed as a new intelligence order, social platforms had already built the behavioral raw material of that order. Their core innovation was not simply communication at scale. It was the normalization of a business model in which attention, relationships, preferences, and expression became persistent, monetizable data assets. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-01-appendix-a1-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); FTC, [FTC Imposes $5 Billion Penalty and Sw...](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2019/07/ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-facebook) (2019-07-24; see [Appendix A2](#ch-01-appendix-a2-ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-and-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-on-facebook))] The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

## Historical or Institutional Context

The FTC's 2024 report on social media and video-streaming data practices gives this chapter its strongest regulatory anchor. The platform model is documented as one of extraction, retention, combination, and monetization, not as a neutral relay of user speech. That record is already enough to weaken the old fiction that sovereign-scale platforms are passive intermediaries. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-01-appendix-a1-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services))]

## Main Analysis

The FTC's 2019 Facebook action matters because Cambridge Analytica was not just a reputational scandal. It became a structural compliance event with board-level oversight duties and a formal federal record of deceptive and insufficiently controlled data governance. ^[Sources: FTC, [FTC Imposes $5 Billion Penalty and Sw...](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2019/07/ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-facebook) (2019-07-24; see [Appendix A2](#ch-01-appendix-a2-ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-and-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-on-facebook))]

Facebook's own `S-1` and later Meta proxy filings document Peter Thiel as an early investor and a long-lived governance actor. That does not prove clandestine command. It does prove continuity between platform surveillance, elite venture governance, and later state-adjacent technology networks. ^[Sources: SEC, [Facebook, Inc. Registration Statement...](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1326801/000119312512034517/d287954ds1.htm) (2012-02-01; see [Appendix A3](#ch-01-appendix-a3-facebook-inc-registration-statement-on-form-s-1)); SEC, [Meta Platforms, Inc. 2022 Definitive...](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1326801/000132680122000043/meta2022definitiveproxysta.htm) (2022-04-08; see [Appendix A4](#ch-01-appendix-a4-meta-platforms-inc-2022-definitive-proxy-statement))]

Meta's 2021 rebrand and 2023 `10-K` show that the company attached a metaverse-centered strategic narrative to a still-dominant advertising machine. The rebrand did not function as a data-governance reset. It functioned as narrative redirection while the extraction engine remained intact and financially central. ^[Sources: Meta, [The Facebook Company Is Now Meta](https://about.fb.com/news/2021/10/facebook-company-is-now-meta/) (2021-10-28; see [Appendix A5](#ch-01-appendix-a5-the-facebook-company-is-now-meta)); U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission, [Meta Platforms, Inc. 2023 Annual Repo...](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1326801/000132680124000012/meta-20231231.htm) (2024-02-02; see [Appendix A6](#ch-01-appendix-a6-meta-platforms-inc-2023-annual-report-form-10-k))]

The documented platform record therefore shows more than large-scale surveillance. It shows social participation being routed through privately governed interfaces that could rank visibility, shape reach, and condition economic opportunity. Interpretation: this is the earlier precedent for the later AI story. Once an intermediary controls the interface layer, it can influence value without producing the underlying social life itself. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-01-appendix-a1-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); FTC, [FTC Imposes $5 Billion Penalty and Sw...](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2019/07/ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-facebook) (2019-07-24; see [Appendix A2](#ch-01-appendix-a2-ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-and-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-on-facebook)); U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission, [Meta Platforms, Inc. 2023 Annual Repo...](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1326801/000132680124000012/meta-20231231.htm) (2024-02-02; see [Appendix A6](#ch-01-appendix-a6-meta-platforms-inc-2023-annual-report-form-10-k))]

Interpretation: Chapter `01` begins the case for `Refactor Section 230` by showing that reach, design, targeting, monetization, and recommendation are not incidental platform features. They are the product. A sovereign-scale platform framework should therefore track systemic design power rather than cling to passive-hosting logic built for an earlier internet. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-01-appendix-a1-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); FTC, [FTC Imposes $5 Billion Penalty and Sw...](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2019/07/ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-facebook) (2019-07-24; see [Appendix A2](#ch-01-appendix-a2-ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-and-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-on-facebook)); U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission, [Meta Platforms, Inc. 2023 Annual Repo...](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1326801/000132680124000012/meta-20231231.htm) (2024-02-02; see [Appendix A6](#ch-01-appendix-a6-meta-platforms-inc-2023-annual-report-form-10-k))]

## Position Within the Series

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest companions are Chapter 02 (Social erosion and moral reconfiguration), Chapter 04 (AI arrives when the human raw material already exists), Chapter 10 (What to do). In that series logic, Chapter 01 builds the structural case for refactoring Section 230 by documenting recommendation, extraction, and design power.

## Counterarguments

This chapter is strongest on surveillance business models, regulatory intervention, governance continuity, and strategic redirection. It is weaker on any maximal claim that every platform dataset was fully mapped into every later foundation-model training stack. That bridge must be built more carefully in Chapter `04`. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-01-appendix-a1-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission, [Meta Platforms, Inc. 2023 Annual Repo...](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1326801/000132680124000012/meta-20231231.htm) (2024-02-02; see [Appendix A6](#ch-01-appendix-a6-meta-platforms-inc-2023-annual-report-form-10-k))]

## Limits of the Evidence

This chapter is strongest on surveillance business models, regulatory intervention, governance continuity, and strategic redirection. It is weaker on any maximal claim that every platform dataset was fully mapped into every later foundation-model training stack. That bridge must be built more carefully in Chapter `04`. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-01-appendix-a1-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission, [Meta Platforms, Inc. 2023 Annual Repo...](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1326801/000132680124000012/meta-20231231.htm) (2024-02-02; see [Appendix A6](#ch-01-appendix-a6-meta-platforms-inc-2023-annual-report-form-10-k))]

## Reform Relevance

Once social life is reorganized around measurable engagement, extractive targeting, and recommendation systems, platforms stop looking like passive infrastructure and start looking like privately governed civic systems. That is why the dossier's Section `230` argument begins here: sovereign-scale platforms should be judged by the harms and dependencies created by their design, amplification, and monetization architecture. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-01-appendix-a1-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); FTC, [FTC Imposes $5 Billion Penalty and Sw...](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2019/07/ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-facebook) (2019-07-24; see [Appendix A2](#ch-01-appendix-a2-ftc-imposes-5-billion-penalty-and-sweeping-new-privacy-restrictions-on-facebook))]

## Conclusion

Once social life is reorganized around measurable engagement, extractive targeting, and recommendation systems, platforms stop looking like passive infrastructure and start looking like privately governed civic systems. The next problem is not only that platforms captured behavior. In the architecture of this series, Chapter 01 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

## Notes

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among `Documented Fact`, `Disputed Fact`, `Hypothesis / Interpretation`, and `Speculative Narrative Risk`. In Chapter 01, those boundaries remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix sections that summarize claims and caution points.

## Appendix E. Evidence Boundaries

### Appendix E1. Disputed Matters {#ch-01-appendix-e1-disputed-matters}

- None registered for this paper.

### Appendix E2. Interpretive Boundaries {#ch-01-appendix-e2-interpretive-boundaries}

- [Appendix B38](#ch-01-appendix-b38-sovereign-scale-platforms-should-be-regulated-on-the-basis-of-design-amplificati): That sovereign-scale platforms should be regulated on the basis of design, amplification, targeting, monetization, moderation architecture, and systemic reach rather than passive-hosting logic.
- [Appendix B39](#ch-01-appendix-b39-the-facebook-thiel-palantir-in-q-tel-record-supports-a-bounded-argument-about-net): That the Facebook-Thiel-Palantir-In-Q-Tel record supports a bounded argument about networked governance continuity between surveillance platforms, venture capital, and security-state technology ecosystems.
- [Appendix B40](#ch-01-appendix-b40-the-strongest-reading-of-the-facebook-thiel-palantir-in-q-tel-record-is-a-structu): That the strongest reading of the Facebook-Thiel-Palantir-In-Q-Tel record is a structured adjacency model built through board power, venture sponsorship, and government contracting rather than evidence of covert command.
- [Appendix B41](#ch-01-appendix-b41-the-meta-rebrand-and-metaverse-cycle-can-be-read-as-a-narrative-and-capital-alloc): That the Meta rebrand and metaverse cycle can be read as a narrative and capital-allocation redirection after a reputational-regulatory rupture, even if the precise intent behind the timing cannot be proved conclusively.

### Appendix E3. Speculative Narrative Risks {#ch-01-appendix-e3-speculative-narrative-risks}

- [Appendix B42](#ch-01-appendix-b42-refactoring-section-230-for-sovereign-scale-platforms-requires-treating-platforms): That refactoring Section 230 for sovereign-scale platforms requires treating platforms as ordinary publishers for every user utterance regardless of design, amplification, targeting, or monetization.
- [Appendix B43](#ch-01-appendix-b43-intelligence-adjacency-or-early-investor-overlap-alone-proves-direct-cia-control): That intelligence adjacency or early-investor overlap alone proves direct CIA control of Facebook.
- [Appendix B44](#ch-01-appendix-b44-the-meta-rebrand-by-itself-proves-a-singular-covert-intent-to-erase-cambridge-ana): That the Meta rebrand by itself proves a singular covert intent to erase Cambridge Analytica from public memory or that the metaverse pivot was primarily a cover-up operation.

- [Appendix A3](#ch-01-appendix-a3-facebook-inc-registration-statement-on-form-s-1). Companion note: Note N-001. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That Peter Thiel had a formal governance role inside Facebook at the time of the IPO filing and that the role dated back to 2005.

# Chapter 02. Social erosion and moral reconfiguration

## Abstract

The platform system did not only capture behavior. The U.S. If attention is trained by systems optimized for engagement rather than flourishing, then the outcome is not only distraction.

## Keywords

Social erosion and moral reconfiguration; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism; Section 230 reform; evidence-based dossier

## Research Question

How does social erosion and moral reconfiguration function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish?

## Scope and Framing Note

This paper examines social erosion and moral reconfiguration within the broader dossier *From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism*. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper that deepens the Section 230 case by connecting amplification systems to youth harm, civic erosion, and moral reconfiguration. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 01, Chapter 03, Chapter 10, but those cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic comprehension.

## Central Thesis

The platform system did not only capture behavior. It also reorganized norms. The strongest version of the claim is not nostalgic moral panic. It is that algorithmic optimization degraded attention, weakened trust, amplified social fragmentation, and altered what appears normal, desirable, and glamorous at scale. ^[Sources: HHS, [Social Media and Youth Mental Health:...](https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-02-appendix-a1-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory)); arXiv, [What Do We Mean When We Talk about Tr...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.03671) (2023-02-07; see [Appendix A2](#ch-02-appendix-a2-what-do-we-mean-when-we-talk-about-trust-in-social-media-a-systematic-review)); arXiv, [Quantifying social organization and p...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.00590) (2020-10-01; see [Appendix A3](#ch-02-appendix-a3-quantifying-social-organization-and-political-polarization-in-online-platforms))]

## Evidence and Method Note

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 02 and is grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

## Introduction

This paper asks: How does social erosion and moral reconfiguration function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer is clear from the start: The platform system did not only capture behavior. It also reorganized norms. The strongest version of the claim is not nostalgic moral panic. It is that algorithmic optimization degraded attention, weakened trust, amplified social fragmentation, and altered what appears normal, desirable, and glamorous at scale. ^[Sources: HHS, [Social Media and Youth Mental Health:...](https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-02-appendix-a1-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory)); arXiv, [What Do We Mean When We Talk about Tr...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.03671) (2023-02-07; see [Appendix A2](#ch-02-appendix-a2-what-do-we-mean-when-we-talk-about-trust-in-social-media-a-systematic-review)); arXiv, [Quantifying social organization and p...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.00590) (2020-10-01; see [Appendix A3](#ch-02-appendix-a3-quantifying-social-organization-and-political-polarization-in-online-platforms))] The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

## Historical or Institutional Context

The U.S. Surgeon General's advisory provides serious institutional backing for discussing cumulative psychosocial risk among young people. That means the youth-harm argument does not rest only on intuition or generational panic. ^[Sources: HHS, [Social Media and Youth Mental Health:...](https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-02-appendix-a1-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory))]

## Main Analysis

The Instagram body-image and TikTok under-13 records show that harms are not confined to public feeds. Private-message dynamics, recommendation mismatches, and child-directed product failures all matter. Platform design is therefore part of the harm structure, not just the venue in which the harm appears. ^[Sources: arXiv, [Unfiltered: How Teens Engage in Body...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02176) (2025-04-02; see [Appendix A4](#ch-02-appendix-a4-unfiltered-how-teens-engage-in-body-image-and-shaming-discussions-via-instagram-direct-messages-dms)); arXiv, [When Kids Mode Isn't For Kids: Invest...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00299) (2025-06-30; see [Appendix A5](#ch-02-appendix-a5-when-kids-mode-isn-t-for-kids-investigating-tiktok-s-under-13-experience))]

The trust and polarization literature strengthens the civic layer. Social media trust, content trust, and user trust are analytically distinct, but all are reshaped by platform architecture. Cross-national Pew reporting adds that large majorities across surveyed countries associate social media with manipulation, division, and reduced civility, while the United States appears as an unusually negative outlier. ^[Sources: arXiv, [What Do We Mean When We Talk about Tr...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.03671) (2023-02-07; see [Appendix A2](#ch-02-appendix-a2-what-do-we-mean-when-we-talk-about-trust-in-social-media-a-systematic-review)); arXiv, [Quantifying social organization and p...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.00590) (2020-10-01; see [Appendix A3](#ch-02-appendix-a3-quantifying-social-organization-and-political-polarization-in-online-platforms)); Pew Research Center, [Social Media Seen as Mostly Good for...](https://www.pewresearch.org/global/2022/12/06/social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier/) (2022-12-06; see [Appendix A6](#ch-02-appendix-a6-social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier))]

The dossier's more morally charged cultural claims also have bounded support. Serious reporting documents recommendation systems intensifying misogynistic content exposure. Research on OnlyFans documents mainstream entry by creators with no prior sex-work history and ties that normalization to platform visibility and pandemic-era adoption. These examples should remain illustrative, but they are not empty rhetoric. ^[Sources: The Guardian, [Social media algorithms 'amplifying m...](https://www.theguardian.com/media/2024/feb/06/social-media-algorithms-amplifying-misogynistic-content) (2024-02-06; see [Appendix A7](#ch-02-appendix-a7-social-media-algorithms-amplifying-misogynistic-content)); arXiv, [Nudes? Shouldn't I charge for these?:...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.10425) (2022-05-20; see [Appendix A8](#ch-02-appendix-a8-nudes-shouldn-t-i-charge-for-these-motivations-of-new-sexual-content-creators-on-onlyfans))]

Interpretation: Chapter `02` deepens the case for `Refactor Section 230` because the social harms here are not reducible to isolated user speech. They emerge from recommendation systems, exposure design, monetized aspiration, and systemic amplification. A sovereign-scale legal framework should therefore focus on design responsibility and measurable harm, not only on moderation after the fact. ^[Sources: HHS, [Social Media and Youth Mental Health:...](https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-02-appendix-a1-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory)); arXiv, [When Kids Mode Isn't For Kids: Invest...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00299) (2025-06-30; see [Appendix A5](#ch-02-appendix-a5-when-kids-mode-isn-t-for-kids-investigating-tiktok-s-under-13-experience)); Pew Research Center, [Social Media Seen as Mostly Good for...](https://www.pewresearch.org/global/2022/12/06/social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier/) (2022-12-06; see [Appendix A6](#ch-02-appendix-a6-social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier))]

## Position Within the Series

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest companions are Chapter 01 (Social networks as infrastructure for free data capture), Chapter 03 (COVID as a historical accelerator), Chapter 10 (What to do). In that series logic, Chapter 02 deepens the Section 230 case by connecting amplification systems to youth harm, civic erosion, and moral reconfiguration.

## Counterarguments

This chapter should not imply that every moral deformation of contemporary life is fully caused by platforms. The evidence is strongest on youth harm, fragmentation, misogynistic amplification, and monetized sexual self-presentation. Broader civilizational claims remain interpretive and should stay visibly bounded. ^[Sources: HHS, [Social Media and Youth Mental Health:...](https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-02-appendix-a1-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory)); The Guardian, [Social media algorithms 'amplifying m...](https://www.theguardian.com/media/2024/feb/06/social-media-algorithms-amplifying-misogynistic-content) (2024-02-06; see [Appendix A7](#ch-02-appendix-a7-social-media-algorithms-amplifying-misogynistic-content))]

## Limits of the Evidence

This chapter should not imply that every moral deformation of contemporary life is fully caused by platforms. The evidence is strongest on youth harm, fragmentation, misogynistic amplification, and monetized sexual self-presentation. Broader civilizational claims remain interpretive and should stay visibly bounded. ^[Sources: HHS, [Social Media and Youth Mental Health:...](https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-02-appendix-a1-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory)); The Guardian, [Social media algorithms 'amplifying m...](https://www.theguardian.com/media/2024/feb/06/social-media-algorithms-amplifying-misogynistic-content) (2024-02-06; see [Appendix A7](#ch-02-appendix-a7-social-media-algorithms-amplifying-misogynistic-content))]

## Reform Relevance

If attention is trained by systems optimized for engagement rather than flourishing, then the outcome is not only distraction. It is a weaker public with thinner trust, more manipulable norms, and reduced civic resilience. That matters because a society softened in this way is easier to govern through later layers of digital mediation and AI dependency. ^[Sources: arXiv, [What Do We Mean When We Talk about Tr...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.03671) (2023-02-07; see [Appendix A2](#ch-02-appendix-a2-what-do-we-mean-when-we-talk-about-trust-in-social-media-a-systematic-review)); arXiv, [Quantifying social organization and p...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.00590) (2020-10-01; see [Appendix A3](#ch-02-appendix-a3-quantifying-social-organization-and-political-polarization-in-online-platforms)); Pew Research Center, [Social Media Seen as Mostly Good for...](https://www.pewresearch.org/global/2022/12/06/social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier/) (2022-12-06; see [Appendix A6](#ch-02-appendix-a6-social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier))]

## Conclusion

If attention is trained by systems optimized for engagement rather than flourishing, then the outcome is not only distraction. The next historical shock did not create this architecture. In the architecture of this series, Chapter 02 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

## Notes

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among `Documented Fact`, `Disputed Fact`, `Hypothesis / Interpretation`, and `Speculative Narrative Risk`. In Chapter 02, those boundaries remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix sections that summarize claims and caution points.

### Appendix E4. Sensitive or Review-Worthy Note Flags {#ch-02-appendix-e4-sensitive-or-review-worthy-note-flags}

- [Appendix A7](#ch-02-appendix-a7-social-media-algorithms-amplifying-misogynistic-content). Companion note: Note N-008. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That serious reporting documents a research finding that recommendation systems can intensify misogynistic content exposure over a short period.
- [Appendix A8](#ch-02-appendix-a8-nudes-shouldn-t-i-charge-for-these-motivations-of-new-sexual-content-creators-on-onlyfans). Companion note: Note N-009. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That mainstream visibility and platform design helped normalize monetized sexual content creation among people with no prior sex-work history.
- [Appendix A1](#ch-02-appendix-a1-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory). Companion note: Note N-010. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That there is serious institutional concern about the impact of social networks on minors.
- [Appendix A4](#ch-02-appendix-a4-unfiltered-how-teens-engage-in-body-image-and-shaming-discussions-via-instagram-direct-messages-dms). Companion note: Note N-011. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That teen body-image discourse on Instagram is not confined to public feeds; it also unfolds in private channels that shape social reinforcement and harm.
- [Appendix A6](#ch-02-appendix-a6-social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier). Companion note: Note N-012. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That cross-national evidence supports the claim that social media is widely associated with manipulation, division, and declining civility, even where some respondents still see democratic benefits.
- [Appendix A5](#ch-02-appendix-a5-when-kids-mode-isn-t-for-kids-investigating-tiktok-s-under-13-experience). Companion note: Note N-013. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That a child-directed platform mode can still expose minors to content that the researchers did not classify as child-directed.
- [Appendix A2](#ch-02-appendix-a2-what-do-we-mean-when-we-talk-about-trust-in-social-media-a-systematic-review). Companion note: Note N-014. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That trust in social media is a serious research problem with enough literature to support systematic synthesis.
- [Appendix A3](#ch-02-appendix-a3-quantifying-social-organization-and-political-polarization-in-online-platforms). Companion note: Note N-015. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That large-scale platform social organization can be measured as fragmented and politically polarized rather than discussed only impressionistically.

# Chapter 03. COVID as a historical accelerator

## Abstract

COVID should be treated as a historical accelerator, not as a proven master plot. The ODNI origin report remains the correct baseline for the most sensitive question. COVID widened the field in which screens became necessary, platforms became indispensable, and digitally mediated life became normalized.

## Keywords

COVID as a historical accelerator; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism; evidence-based dossier

## Research Question

How does covid as a historical accelerator function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish?

## Scope and Framing Note

This paper examines covid as a historical accelerator within the broader dossier *From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism*. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper that shows how crisis conditions accelerated digital dependence and normalized the infrastructure later challenged by the reform agenda. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 01, Chapter 02, Chapter 04, but those cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic comprehension.

## Central Thesis

COVID should be treated as a historical accelerator, not as a proven master plot. The strongest documentary case is that the pandemic consolidated digital dependency, normalized algorithmic mediation, and advantaged dominant platforms in ways that outlasted the emergency itself. ^[Sources: ODNI, [Report on Potential Links Between the...](https://www.dni.gov/files/ODNI/documents/assessments/Report-on-Potential-Links-Between-the-Wuhan-Institute-of-Virology-and-the-Origins-of-COVID-19-20230623.pdf) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-03-appendix-a1-report-on-potential-links-between-the-wuhan-institute-of-virology-and-the-origins-of-covid-19)); TIME, [Big Tech Announces Striking Pandemic...](https://time.com/6085674/big-tech-apple-microsoft-facebook-amazon-google-earnings/) (2021-08-02; see [Appendix A2](#ch-03-appendix-a2-big-tech-announces-striking-pandemic-gains-as-small-businesses-strain-to-find-their-footing)); Axios, [Pandemic provides a techlash reprieve](https://www.axios.com/2021/03/10/pandemic-provides-techlash-reprieve) (2021-03-10; see [Appendix A3](#ch-03-appendix-a3-pandemic-provides-a-techlash-reprieve))]

## Evidence and Method Note

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 03 and is grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

## Introduction

This paper asks: How does covid as a historical accelerator function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer is clear from the start: COVID should be treated as a historical accelerator, not as a proven master plot. The strongest documentary case is that the pandemic consolidated digital dependency, normalized algorithmic mediation, and advantaged dominant platforms in ways that outlasted the emergency itself. ^[Sources: ODNI, [Report on Potential Links Between the...](https://www.dni.gov/files/ODNI/documents/assessments/Report-on-Potential-Links-Between-the-Wuhan-Institute-of-Virology-and-the-Origins-of-COVID-19-20230623.pdf) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-03-appendix-a1-report-on-potential-links-between-the-wuhan-institute-of-virology-and-the-origins-of-covid-19)); TIME, [Big Tech Announces Striking Pandemic...](https://time.com/6085674/big-tech-apple-microsoft-facebook-amazon-google-earnings/) (2021-08-02; see [Appendix A2](#ch-03-appendix-a2-big-tech-announces-striking-pandemic-gains-as-small-businesses-strain-to-find-their-footing)); Axios, [Pandemic provides a techlash reprieve](https://www.axios.com/2021/03/10/pandemic-provides-techlash-reprieve) (2021-03-10; see [Appendix A3](#ch-03-appendix-a3-pandemic-provides-a-techlash-reprieve))] The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

## Historical or Institutional Context

The ODNI origin report remains the correct baseline for the most sensitive question. It supports a disciplined conclusion: the origin of COVID-19 remained officially disputed and unresolved. That means the dossier can discuss the pandemic's geopolitical implications, but it cannot honestly present a totalized certainty claim about deliberate design or a proved Cold War operation. ^[Sources: ODNI, [Report on Potential Links Between the...](https://www.dni.gov/files/ODNI/documents/assessments/Report-on-Potential-Links-Between-the-Wuhan-Institute-of-Virology-and-the-Origins-of-COVID-19-20230623.pdf) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-03-appendix-a1-report-on-potential-links-between-the-wuhan-institute-of-virology-and-the-origins-of-covid-19))]

## Main Analysis

The evidence for acceleration is much stronger. Research on work-from-home durability, remote collaboration, and pandemic-era privacy risk shows that digital mediation was not merely a temporary improvisation. It reshaped institutional life and normalized new forms of oversight and dependence. ^[Sources: arXiv, [Work-From-Home is Here to Stay: Call...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.11136) (2022-03-21; see [Appendix A4](#ch-03-appendix-a4-work-from-home-is-here-to-stay-call-for-flexibility-in-post-pandemic-work-policies)); arXiv, [How Work From Home Affects Collaborat...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.15584) (2020-07-30; see [Appendix A5](#ch-03-appendix-a5-how-work-from-home-affects-collaboration-a-large-scale-study-of-information-workers-in-a-natural-experiment-during-covid-19)); arXiv, [Remote Working Pre- and Post-COVID-19...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2107.03907) (2021-07-08; see [Appendix A6](#ch-03-appendix-a6-remote-working-pre-and-post-covid-19-an-analysis-of-new-threats-and-risks-to-security-and-privacy))]

The social layer widened as well. COVID-era research and reporting show that digital platforms became central to health information, misinformation management, social interpretation, and everyday family life. Household and youth screen-time evidence makes that intensification measurable rather than anecdotal. ^[Sources: arXiv, [What social media told about us in th...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2101.01688) (2021-01-05; see [Appendix A7](#ch-03-appendix-a7-what-social-media-told-about-us-in-the-time-of-covid-19-a-scoping-review)); arXiv, [Excessive Screen Time is Associated w...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10062) (2025-08-13; see [Appendix A8](#ch-03-appendix-a8-excessive-screen-time-is-associated-with-mental-health-problems-and-adhd-in-us-children-and-adolescents-physical-activity-and-sleep-as-parallel-mediators)); Axios, [Kids' screen time up 50% during pandemic](https://www.axios.com/2021/01/17/kids-screen-time-pandemic) (2021-01-17; see [Appendix A9](#ch-03-appendix-a9-kids-screen-time-up-50-during-pandemic))]

The concentration story also became clearer. Pandemic-era gains for large tech firms diverged sharply from small-business strain, and serious reporting documented a temporary reprieve from the pre-COVID techlash because the same firms under scrutiny had become indispensable infrastructure for work, school, commerce, and communication. ^[Sources: TIME, [Big Tech Announces Striking Pandemic...](https://time.com/6085674/big-tech-apple-microsoft-facebook-amazon-google-earnings/) (2021-08-02; see [Appendix A2](#ch-03-appendix-a2-big-tech-announces-striking-pandemic-gains-as-small-businesses-strain-to-find-their-footing)); Axios, [Pandemic provides a techlash reprieve](https://www.axios.com/2021/03/10/pandemic-provides-techlash-reprieve) (2021-03-10; see [Appendix A3](#ch-03-appendix-a3-pandemic-provides-a-techlash-reprieve))]

Interpretation: once the effects are separated from the unresolved origin question, a geopolitical reading becomes possible but must remain explicitly interpretive. The pandemic functioned like a force multiplier for mediation, dependence, and concentration even if the deeper origin story remains unresolved. ^[Sources: ODNI, [Report on Potential Links Between the...](https://www.dni.gov/files/ODNI/documents/assessments/Report-on-Potential-Links-Between-the-Wuhan-Institute-of-Virology-and-the-Origins-of-COVID-19-20230623.pdf) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-03-appendix-a1-report-on-potential-links-between-the-wuhan-institute-of-virology-and-the-origins-of-covid-19)); arXiv, [Work-From-Home is Here to Stay: Call...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.11136) (2022-03-21; see [Appendix A4](#ch-03-appendix-a4-work-from-home-is-here-to-stay-call-for-flexibility-in-post-pandemic-work-policies)); TIME, [Big Tech Announces Striking Pandemic...](https://time.com/6085674/big-tech-apple-microsoft-facebook-amazon-google-earnings/) (2021-08-02; see [Appendix A2](#ch-03-appendix-a2-big-tech-announces-striking-pandemic-gains-as-small-businesses-strain-to-find-their-footing))]

## Position Within the Series

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest companions are Chapter 01 (Social networks as infrastructure for free data capture), Chapter 02 (Social erosion and moral reconfiguration), Chapter 04 (AI arrives when the human raw material already exists). In that series logic, Chapter 03 shows how crisis conditions accelerated digital dependence and normalized the infrastructure later challenged by the reform agenda.

## Counterarguments

The evidence strongly supports acceleration, dependence, and concentration. It does not support presenting the pandemic as a fully demonstrated act of coordinated strategic design. That stronger claim remains outside the documentary core unless much stronger evidence emerges. ^[Sources: ODNI, [Report on Potential Links Between the...](https://www.dni.gov/files/ODNI/documents/assessments/Report-on-Potential-Links-Between-the-Wuhan-Institute-of-Virology-and-the-Origins-of-COVID-19-20230623.pdf) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-03-appendix-a1-report-on-potential-links-between-the-wuhan-institute-of-virology-and-the-origins-of-covid-19))]

## Limits of the Evidence

The evidence strongly supports acceleration, dependence, and concentration. It does not support presenting the pandemic as a fully demonstrated act of coordinated strategic design. That stronger claim remains outside the documentary core unless much stronger evidence emerges. ^[Sources: ODNI, [Report on Potential Links Between the...](https://www.dni.gov/files/ODNI/documents/assessments/Report-on-Potential-Links-Between-the-Wuhan-Institute-of-Virology-and-the-Origins-of-COVID-19-20230623.pdf) (n.d.; see [Appendix A1](#ch-03-appendix-a1-report-on-potential-links-between-the-wuhan-institute-of-virology-and-the-origins-of-covid-19))]

## Reform Relevance

COVID widened the field in which screens became necessary, platforms became indispensable, and digitally mediated life became normalized. That matters because the next phase of generative AI arrived not into a free population but into a population already habituated to living through interfaces. ^[Sources: arXiv, [Work-From-Home is Here to Stay: Call...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.11136) (2022-03-21; see [Appendix A4](#ch-03-appendix-a4-work-from-home-is-here-to-stay-call-for-flexibility-in-post-pandemic-work-policies)); arXiv, [What social media told about us in th...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2101.01688) (2021-01-05; see [Appendix A7](#ch-03-appendix-a7-what-social-media-told-about-us-in-the-time-of-covid-19-a-scoping-review)); Axios, [Kids' screen time up 50% during pandemic](https://www.axios.com/2021/01/17/kids-screen-time-pandemic) (2021-01-17; see [Appendix A9](#ch-03-appendix-a9-kids-screen-time-up-50-during-pandemic))]

## Conclusion

COVID widened the field in which screens became necessary, platforms became indispensable, and digitally mediated life became normalized. By the time frontier AI arrives at scale, the key precondition is already in place: the human raw material, and much of the dependence needed to exploit it, has already been captured. In the architecture of this series, Chapter 03 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

## Notes

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among `Documented Fact`, `Disputed Fact`, `Hypothesis / Interpretation`, and `Speculative Narrative Risk`. In Chapter 03, those boundaries remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix sections that summarize claims and caution points.

# Chapter 04. AI arrives when the human raw material already exists

## Abstract

Foundation models do not emerge from nowhere. The U.S. Once captured human production can be recombined into proprietary weights, the surveillance economy acquires a new layer.

## Keywords

AI arrives when the human raw material already exists; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism; AI weights; patrimony of humanity; evidence-based dossier

## Research Question

How does ai arrives when the human raw material already exists function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish?

## Scope and Framing Note

This paper examines ai arrives when the human raw material already exists within the broader dossier *From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism*. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper that provides the central evidentiary basis for treating frontier weights as a patrimony and governance problem rather than a neutral market outcome. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 00, Chapter 05, Chapter 06, Chapter 10, but those cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic comprehension.

## Central Thesis

Foundation models do not emerge from nowhere. They emerge after a long period in which human expression, cultural production, and socially filtered judgment were captured at scale, weakly compensated if at all, and converted into machine-trainable inputs. ^[Sources: U.S. Copyright Office, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf) (2025-05-09; see [Appendix A1](#ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version)); OpenAI, [Language Models are Unsupervised Mult...](https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf) (2019-02-14; see [Appendix A2](#ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners)); AP, [Reddit strikes $60M deal allowing Goo...](https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708) (2024-02-22; see [Appendix A3](#ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans))]

## Evidence and Method Note

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 04 and is grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

## Introduction

This paper asks: How does ai arrives when the human raw material already exists function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer is clear from the start: Foundation models do not emerge from nowhere. They emerge after a long period in which human expression, cultural production, and socially filtered judgment were captured at scale, weakly compensated if at all, and converted into machine-trainable inputs. ^[Sources: U.S. Copyright Office, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf) (2025-05-09; see [Appendix A1](#ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version)); OpenAI, [Language Models are Unsupervised Mult...](https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf) (2019-02-14; see [Appendix A2](#ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners)); AP, [Reddit strikes $60M deal allowing Goo...](https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708) (2024-02-22; see [Appendix A3](#ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans))] The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

## Historical or Institutional Context

The U.S. Copyright Office has already treated AI training on copyrighted works as a major live policy problem rather than a settled technical inevitability. The 2023 inquiry and 2025 report together show that training-input legality, disclosure, and compensation were contested near the start of the generative-AI boom and remain contested now. ^[Sources: GovInfo, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2023-08-30/pdf/2023-18624.pdf) (2023-08-30; see [Appendix A4](#ch-04-appendix-a4-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-notice-of-inquiry)); U.S. Copyright Office, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf) (2025-05-09; see [Appendix A1](#ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version))]

## Main Analysis

The technical and legal record also undermines the industry's preferred abstraction. Research on memorization shows that large language models can reproduce identifiable material from training data, while court records and legal scholarship now separate at least two layers of conflict: training-use doctrine and input-acquisition legality. ^[Sources: arXiv, [Extracting Training Data from Large L...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.07805) (2020-12-14; see [Appendix A5](#ch-04-appendix-a5-extracting-training-data-from-large-language-models)); arXiv, [Generative AI Training and Copyright Law](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15858) (2025-02-21; see [Appendix A6](#ch-04-appendix-a6-generative-ai-training-and-copyright-law)); U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California via CourtListener, [Bartz et al. v. Anthropic PBC, Order...](https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.cand.434709/gov.uscourts.cand.434709.231.0_3.pdf) (2025-06-23; see [Appendix A7](#ch-04-appendix-a7-bartz-et-al-v-anthropic-pbc-order-on-fair-use)); U.S. District Court for the Southern District of New York via Justia, [The New York Times Company v. Microso...](https://cases.justia.com/federal/district-courts/new-york/nysdce/1%3A2023cv11195/612697/514/0.pdf?ts=1743868265) (2025-04-04; see [Appendix A8](#ch-04-appendix-a8-the-new-york-times-company-v-microsoft-corporation-et-al-opinion-on-motions-to-dismiss))]

The social-to-model bridge is also explicit. GPT-2's WebText was built from socially filtered outbound links shared on Reddit, and by February 2024 Reddit had moved from diffuse public conversation to direct licensing, striking a major agreement that allowed Google to use Reddit posts for AI training and related product improvement. The extraction economy did not end with unpaid capture; it evolved into direct licensing over accumulated human expression. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [Language Models are Unsupervised Mult...](https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf) (2019-02-14; see [Appendix A2](#ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners)); AP, [Reddit strikes $60M deal allowing Goo...](https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708) (2024-02-22; see [Appendix A3](#ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans))]

The labor layer matters too. Research on annotation and outsourced human labor shows that model building depends not only on scraped creative works but also on significant human verification, correction, and operational support that is often geographically externalized and weakly valued. ^[Sources: Big Data & Society, [The problem with annotation. Human la...](https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/20539517231188723) (2023-07-25; see [Appendix A9](#ch-04-appendix-a9-the-problem-with-annotation-human-labour-and-outsourcing-between-france-and-madagascar))]

Taken together, the record shows a sequence in which collectively produced expression becomes training input, archived conversation becomes licensable model fuel, and additional human labor is used to clean, annotate, and operationalize the resulting systems. Value that first appeared as diffuse public production is progressively reorganized inside proprietary model pipelines. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [Language Models are Unsupervised Mult...](https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf) (2019-02-14; see [Appendix A2](#ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners)); AP, [Reddit strikes $60M deal allowing Goo...](https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708) (2024-02-22; see [Appendix A3](#ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans)); Big Data & Society, [The problem with annotation. Human la...](https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/20539517231188723) (2023-07-25; see [Appendix A9](#ch-04-appendix-a9-the-problem-with-annotation-human-labour-and-outsourcing-between-france-and-madagascar))]

Interpretation: the strategic move is not only model creation but control of the interface through which machine-packaged intelligence is accessed. In that reading, the shelf is the interface: the prompt box, chatbot, or subscription layer becomes the first point of contact between a buyer and capability distilled from human work. Once that first contact is captured, value can be repriced downward and dependence can deepen even where total technical replacement remains incomplete. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [Language Models are Unsupervised Mult...](https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf) (2019-02-14; see [Appendix A2](#ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners)); AP, [Reddit strikes $60M deal allowing Goo...](https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708) (2024-02-22; see [Appendix A3](#ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans)); Big Data & Society, [The problem with annotation. Human la...](https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/20539517231188723) (2023-07-25; see [Appendix A9](#ch-04-appendix-a9-the-problem-with-annotation-human-labour-and-outsourcing-between-france-and-madagascar))]

The House AI Task Force report gives this chapter an institutional bridge beyond agencies and courts. By December 17, 2024, a bipartisan congressional report was already recording the training conflict in language overlapping with the dossier's core concerns about commercialization, consent, credit, compensation, and derivative-work theory. ^[Sources: U.S. House of Representatives, [Bipartisan House Task Force Report on...](https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf) (2024-12-17; see [Appendix A10](#ch-04-appendix-a10-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence)); TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A11](#ch-04-appendix-a11-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law))]

Interpretation: this is the dossier's primary evidentiary base for `AI Weights as Patrimony of Humanity`. If frontier weights are built from collectively produced human expression and labor, then they cannot be treated as ordinary private assets by default. The strongest normative direction pushes toward broad, open, utility-like availability under public-interest rules rather than permanent private scarcity. That is advocated doctrine, not settled law. ^[Sources: TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A11](#ch-04-appendix-a11-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law)); U.S. Copyright Office, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf) (2025-05-09; see [Appendix A1](#ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version)); U.S. House of Representatives, [Bipartisan House Task Force Report on...](https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf) (2024-12-17; see [Appendix A10](#ch-04-appendix-a10-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence))]

## Position Within the Series

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest companions are Chapter 00 (Opening framework), Chapter 05 (Talent, overhiring, valuation, and layoffs), Chapter 06 (AI as a dual-use rhetorical weapon), Chapter 10 (What to do). In that series logic, Chapter 04 provides the central evidentiary basis for treating frontier weights as a patrimony and governance problem rather than a neutral market outcome.

## Counterarguments

The current record is strong on conflict, appropriation structure, memorization, licensing, and the social-to-model bridge. It is still weaker on full training-stack provenance across every frontier lab. This chapter should therefore challenge enclosure forcefully without pretending that every corpus is already fully mapped. ^[Sources: U.S. Copyright Office, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf) (2025-05-09; see [Appendix A1](#ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version)); U.S. District Court for the Southern District of New York via Justia, [The New York Times Company v. Microso...](https://cases.justia.com/federal/district-courts/new-york/nysdce/1%3A2023cv11195/612697/514/0.pdf?ts=1743868265) (2025-04-04; see [Appendix A8](#ch-04-appendix-a8-the-new-york-times-company-v-microsoft-corporation-et-al-opinion-on-motions-to-dismiss))]

## Limits of the Evidence

The current record is strong on conflict, appropriation structure, memorization, licensing, and the social-to-model bridge. It is still weaker on full training-stack provenance across every frontier lab. This chapter should therefore challenge enclosure forcefully without pretending that every corpus is already fully mapped. ^[Sources: U.S. Copyright Office, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf) (2025-05-09; see [Appendix A1](#ch-04-appendix-a1-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version)); U.S. District Court for the Southern District of New York via Justia, [The New York Times Company v. Microso...](https://cases.justia.com/federal/district-courts/new-york/nysdce/1%3A2023cv11195/612697/514/0.pdf?ts=1743868265) (2025-04-04; see [Appendix A8](#ch-04-appendix-a8-the-new-york-times-company-v-microsoft-corporation-et-al-opinion-on-motions-to-dismiss))]

## Reform Relevance

Once captured human production can be recombined into proprietary weights, the surveillance economy acquires a new layer. It no longer only predicts behavior. It begins to substitute for the producers whose accumulated traces made the system possible in the first place. That is why the ownership question has to expand into a governance question. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [Language Models are Unsupervised Mult...](https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf) (2019-02-14; see [Appendix A2](#ch-04-appendix-a2-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners)); AP, [Reddit strikes $60M deal allowing Goo...](https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708) (2024-02-22; see [Appendix A3](#ch-04-appendix-a3-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans)); TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A11](#ch-04-appendix-a11-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law))]

## Conclusion

Once captured human production can be recombined into proprietary weights, the surveillance economy acquires a new layer. The next question is how that enclosure story meets labor, finance, and layoffs. In the architecture of this series, Chapter 04 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

## Notes

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among `Documented Fact`, `Disputed Fact`, `Hypothesis / Interpretation`, and `Speculative Narrative Risk`. In Chapter 04, those boundaries remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix sections that summarize claims and caution points.
