# Chapter 08. Power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture

## Abstract

Frontier firms do not consolidate power by product alone. OpenAI publicly treated the November 2023 crisis as a governance rupture and responded with a visible legitimacy-repair process that included board reset, formal review, and later restructuring. Once legitimacy is institutionally assembled, frontier AI stops looking like just another industry and begins to look like a state-adjacent governing infrastructure.

## Keywords

Power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism; government capture; evidence-based dossier

## Research Question

How does power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish?

## Scope and Framing Note

This paper examines power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture within the broader dossier *From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism*. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper that shows how access is legitimized, stabilized, and normalized through governance, lobbying, elite networks, and institutional prestige. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 07, Chapter 09, Chapter 10, but those cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic comprehension.

## Central Thesis

Frontier firms do not consolidate power by product alone. They do so by building legitimacy around the product. That legitimacy is assembled through governance form, elite board composition, security credentials, policy access, operational state interfaces, philanthropy, and political spending. The result is not necessarily a secret cabal. It is a documented field of mutual reinforcement through ordinary institutions. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [OpenAI announces leadership transition](https://openai.com/index/openai-announces-leadership-transition/) (2023-11-17; see [Appendix A1](#ch-08-appendix-a1-openai-announces-leadership-transition)); Associated Press, [OpenAI appoints former top US cyberwa...](https://apnews.com/article/6ef612a3a0fcaef05480bbd1ebbd79b1) (2024-06-14; see [Appendix A2](#ch-08-appendix-a2-openai-appoints-former-top-us-cyberwarrior-paul-nakasone-to-its-board-of-directors)); Axios, [Anthropic outspends OpenAI in biggest...](https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter) (2026-04-21; see [Appendix A3](#ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter))]

## Evidence and Method Note

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 08 and is grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

## Introduction

This paper asks: How does power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer is clear from the start: Frontier firms do not consolidate power by product alone. They do so by building legitimacy around the product. That legitimacy is assembled through governance form, elite board composition, security credentials, policy access, operational state interfaces, philanthropy, and political spending. The result is not necessarily a secret cabal. It is a documented field of mutual reinforcement through ordinary institutions. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [OpenAI announces leadership transition](https://openai.com/index/openai-announces-leadership-transition/) (2023-11-17; see [Appendix A1](#ch-08-appendix-a1-openai-announces-leadership-transition)); Associated Press, [OpenAI appoints former top US cyberwa...](https://apnews.com/article/6ef612a3a0fcaef05480bbd1ebbd79b1) (2024-06-14; see [Appendix A2](#ch-08-appendix-a2-openai-appoints-former-top-us-cyberwarrior-paul-nakasone-to-its-board-of-directors)); Axios, [Anthropic outspends OpenAI in biggest...](https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter) (2026-04-21; see [Appendix A3](#ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter))] The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

## Historical or Institutional Context

OpenAI publicly treated the November 2023 crisis as a governance rupture and responded with a visible legitimacy-repair process that included board reset, formal review, and later restructuring. That shows legitimacy was not incidental to frontier status. It had to be actively rebuilt. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [OpenAI announces leadership transition](https://openai.com/index/openai-announces-leadership-transition/) (2023-11-17; see [Appendix A1](#ch-08-appendix-a1-openai-announces-leadership-transition)); OpenAI, [OpenAI announces new members to board...](https://openai.com/index/openai-announces-new-members-to-board-of-directors/) (2024-03-08; see [Appendix A4](#ch-08-appendix-a4-openai-announces-new-members-to-board-of-directors)); OpenAI, [OpenAI board forms safety and securit...](https://openai.com/index/openai-board-forms-safety-and-security-committee/) (2024-05-28; see [Appendix A5](#ch-08-appendix-a5-openai-board-forms-safety-and-security-committee))]

## Main Analysis

The company's later appointments and committee structures show legitimacy being rebuilt through establishment credentials as much as technical authority. Security and defense credibility became part of the governance story. ^[Sources: Associated Press, [OpenAI appoints former top US cyberwa...](https://apnews.com/article/6ef612a3a0fcaef05480bbd1ebbd79b1) (2024-06-14; see [Appendix A2](#ch-08-appendix-a2-openai-appoints-former-top-us-cyberwarrior-paul-nakasone-to-its-board-of-directors)); OpenAI, [OpenAI board forms safety and securit...](https://openai.com/index/openai-board-forms-safety-and-security-committee/) (2024-05-28; see [Appendix A5](#ch-08-appendix-a5-openai-board-forms-safety-and-security-committee)); Financial Times, [OpenAI appoints one of Wall Street's...](https://www.ft.com/content/63b08a9d-e537-4d60-9904-c59958a16982) (2025-01-14; see [Appendix A6](#ch-08-appendix-a6-openai-appoints-one-of-wall-street-s-most-powerful-dealmakers-to-its-board))]

The policy and state-interface layers described in Chapter `07` matter differently here. `OpenAI for Government`, `Claude Gov`, classified-distribution channels, and `Detachment 201` show how privileged state access can be normalized as routine institutional partnership rather than treated as an exceptional concentration of power. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [Introducing OpenAI for Government](https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/) (2025-06-16; see [Appendix A7](#ch-08-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government)); Anthropic, [Claude Gov models for U.S. national s...](https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers) (2025-06-06; see [Appendix A8](#ch-08-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers)); Palantir Technologies, [Anthropic and Palantir Partner to Bri...](https://investors.palantir.com/news-details/2024/Anthropic-and-Palantir-Partner-to-Bring-Claude-AI-Models-to-AWS-for-U.S.-Government-Intelligence-and-Defense-Operations/) (2024-11-07; see [Appendix A9](#ch-08-appendix-a9-anthropic-and-palantir-partner-to-bring-claude-ai-models-to-aws-for-u-s-government-intelligence-and-defense-operations)); U.S. Army Public Affairs, [Army Launches Detachment 201: Executi...](https://www.army.mil/article/286317/army_launches_detachment_201_executive_innovation_corps_to_drive_tech_transformation) (2025-06-13; see [Appendix A10](#ch-08-appendix-a10-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation))]

The influence layer widened by 2026. Lobbying disclosures and PAC records show that frontier legitimacy expanded beyond public messaging into formal Washington and electoral infrastructure. That means legitimacy was not only reputational. It was procedural and political. ^[Sources: Axios, [Anthropic outspends OpenAI in biggest...](https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter) (2026-04-21; see [Appendix A3](#ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter)); Federal Election Commission, [LEADING THE FUTURE - committee overview](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00916114/) (2026-03-31; see [Appendix A11](#ch-08-appendix-a11-leading-the-future-committee-overview))]

The moral-authority layer widened too. Dario Amodei publicly warned that AI-driven wealth concentration could destabilize democracy while also describing the founders' 80% wealth pledge. The Vatican's 2026 encyclical shows that the legitimacy field had expanded beyond industry and policy into formal civilizational and religious authority. These sources do not absolve frontier firms. They show how moral positioning becomes part of the legitimacy field itself. ^[Sources: Dario Amodei, [The Adolescence of Technology](https://darioamodei.com/essay/the-adolescence-of-technology) (2026-01-01; see [Appendix A12](#ch-08-appendix-a12-the-adolescence-of-technology)); The Holy See, [Encyclical Letter of His Holiness Leo...](https://www.vatican.va/content/leo-xiv/en/encyclicals/documents/20260515-magnifica-humanitas.html) (2026-05-15; see [Appendix A13](#ch-08-appendix-a13-encyclical-letter-of-his-holiness-leo-xiv-magnifica-humanitas))]

Another part of the legitimacy problem is private survivability hedging. In 2016, Sam Altman publicly described storing guns, gold, potassium iodide, antibiotics, gas masks, and having a New Zealand fallback plan tied to Peter Thiel. In 2023, Wired documented Mark Zuckerberg's Kauai compound as including an underground shelter, secrecy controls on construction crews, and self-sufficiency features; in 2024, Zuckerberg publicly minimized the structure as "just a little shelter." None of this proves that these actors expect imminent collapse or are engineering it. But it does sharpen a bounded political question: legitimacy weakens when elites ask publics and states to normalize dependency on systems they govern while privately investing in exemption from shared risk. ^[Sources: Tad Friend, [Sam Altman's Manifest Destiny](https://www.newyorker.com/magazine/2016/10/10/sam-altmans-manifest-destiny) (2016-10-03; see [Appendix A14](#ch-08-appendix-a14-sam-altman-s-manifest-destiny)); Makena Kelly, [Inside Mark Zuckerberg’s Top-Secret H...](https://www.wired.com/story/mark-zuckerberg-inside-hawaii-compound) (2023-12-14; see [Appendix A15](#ch-08-appendix-a15-inside-mark-zuckerbergs-top-secret-hawaii-compound)); Charlotte Phillipp, [Mark Zuckerberg Downplays His 5,000-S...](https://people.com/mark-zuckerberg-hawaii-bunker-5000-square-feet-8765376) (2024-12-20; see [Appendix A16](#ch-08-appendix-a16-mark-zuckerberg-downplays-his-5-000-square-foot-underground-bunker-in-hawaii-just-a-little-shelter))]

Named-individual material belongs here only when it clarifies institutional control, mission conflict, or legal architecture. The Musk-OpenAI litigation matters because it shows that nonprofit mission, restructuring, and governance control became a formal legal battleground. The AP injunction-denial reporting further shows that the dispute remained live even after emergency relief was denied. ^[Sources: CourtListener / PACER, [Musk v. Altman, 4:24-cv-04722](https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69013420/musk-v-altman/) (2024-08-05; see [Appendix A17](#ch-08-appendix-a17-musk-v-altman-4-24-cv-04722)); Matt O'Brien; Barbara Ortutay, [Judge denies Elon Musk's request to b...](https://apnews.com/article/elon-musk-openai-lawsuit-f5724e7ab07b5bed8292a1e8aa2ef695) (2025-03-05; see [Appendix A18](#ch-08-appendix-a18-judge-denies-elon-musk-s-request-to-block-openai-for-profit-conversion-but-welcomes-trial)); Financial Times, [Judge denies Elon Musk’s attempt to i...](https://www.ft.com/content/532931dd-e1c0-4f18-a8af-5777dd70a52d) (2025-03-05; see [Appendix A19](#ch-08-appendix-a19-judge-denies-elon-musks-attempt-to-immediately-block-openais-conversion-to-for-profit-entity))]

Interpretation: this chapter builds the structural case for `Limit Government Capture by AI Companies`. The strongest reading is not hidden sovereign command. It is that governance form, security credentials, policy access, lobbying, philanthropy, and operational state interfaces together normalize extraordinary concentration before democratic controls catch up. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [OpenAI’s proposals for the U.S. AI Ac...](https://openai.com/global-affairs/openai-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan/) (2025-03-13; see [Appendix A20](#ch-08-appendix-a20-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan)); OpenAI, [Introducing OpenAI for Government](https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/) (2025-06-16; see [Appendix A7](#ch-08-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government)); Axios, [Anthropic outspends OpenAI in biggest...](https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter) (2026-04-21; see [Appendix A3](#ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter)); Dario Amodei, [The Adolescence of Technology](https://darioamodei.com/essay/the-adolescence-of-technology) (2026-01-01; see [Appendix A12](#ch-08-appendix-a12-the-adolescence-of-technology))]

## Position Within the Series

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest companions are Chapter 07 (Imperial control over access), Chapter 09 (Future if no action is taken), Chapter 10 (What to do). In that series logic, Chapter 08 shows how access is legitimized, stabilized, and normalized through governance, lobbying, elite networks, and institutional prestige.

## Counterarguments

The chapter must preserve a crucial distinction: elite overlap and influence channels do not by themselves prove unified hidden control. The record is strongest on formal influence, legitimacy repair, disclosed interfaces, and institutional reinforcement, not on secret ownership of every outcome. ^[Sources: CourtListener / PACER, [Musk v. Altman, 4:24-cv-04722](https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69013420/musk-v-altman/) (2024-08-05; see [Appendix A17](#ch-08-appendix-a17-musk-v-altman-4-24-cv-04722)); Federal Election Commission, [LEADING THE FUTURE - committee overview](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00916114/) (2026-03-31; see [Appendix A11](#ch-08-appendix-a11-leading-the-future-committee-overview))]

## Limits of the Evidence

The chapter must preserve a crucial distinction: elite overlap and influence channels do not by themselves prove unified hidden control. The record is strongest on formal influence, legitimacy repair, disclosed interfaces, and institutional reinforcement, not on secret ownership of every outcome. ^[Sources: CourtListener / PACER, [Musk v. Altman, 4:24-cv-04722](https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69013420/musk-v-altman/) (2024-08-05; see [Appendix A17](#ch-08-appendix-a17-musk-v-altman-4-24-cv-04722)); Federal Election Commission, [LEADING THE FUTURE - committee overview](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00916114/) (2026-03-31; see [Appendix A11](#ch-08-appendix-a11-leading-the-future-committee-overview))]

## Reform Relevance

Once legitimacy is institutionally assembled, frontier AI stops looking like just another industry and begins to look like a state-adjacent governing infrastructure. That is why any material government relationship with frontier firms should trigger transparency, conflict-of-interest controls, auditability, procurement visibility, and limits on exclusive private benefit. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [Introducing OpenAI for Government](https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/) (2025-06-16; see [Appendix A7](#ch-08-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government)); Anthropic, [Claude Gov models for U.S. national s...](https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers) (2025-06-06; see [Appendix A8](#ch-08-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers)); U.S. Army Public Affairs, [Army Launches Detachment 201: Executi...](https://www.army.mil/article/286317/army_launches_detachment_201_executive_innovation_corps_to_drive_tech_transformation) (2025-06-13; see [Appendix A10](#ch-08-appendix-a10-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation)); Axios, [Anthropic outspends OpenAI in biggest...](https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter) (2026-04-21; see [Appendix A3](#ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter))]

## Conclusion

Once legitimacy is institutionally assembled, frontier AI stops looking like just another industry and begins to look like a state-adjacent governing infrastructure. At that point the dossier can ask its final scenario question: if the present trajectory continues, what kind of social order does it produce? In the architecture of this series, Chapter 08 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

## Notes

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among `Documented Fact`, `Disputed Fact`, `Hypothesis / Interpretation`, and `Speculative Narrative Risk`. In Chapter 08, those boundaries remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix sections that summarize claims and caution points.

## Appendix E. Evidence Boundaries

### Appendix E1. Disputed Matters {#ch-08-appendix-e1-disputed-matters}

- [Appendix B66](#ch-08-appendix-b66-the-precise-degree-of-direct-openai-alignment-behind-leading-the-future-remains-c): That the precise degree of direct OpenAI alignment behind `LEADING THE FUTURE` remains contested beyond the current FEC filing record and should remain bounded to the Axios reporting layer.
- [Appendix B67](#ch-08-appendix-b67-the-public-record-does-not-settle-whether-q-or-any-specific-capability-breakthro): That the public record does not settle whether Q* or any specific capability breakthrough directly triggered the November 2023 OpenAI board rupture.
- [Appendix B68](#ch-08-appendix-b68-allegations-advanced-by-named-actors-in-openai-governance-disputes-are-procedural): That allegations advanced by named actors in OpenAI governance disputes are procedurally live and politically relevant without being established as true.

### Appendix E2. Interpretive Boundaries {#ch-08-appendix-e2-interpretive-boundaries}

- [Appendix B69](#ch-08-appendix-b69-current-official-records-show-frontier-labs-and-adjacent-platform-firms-building): That current official records show frontier labs and adjacent platform firms building formal government-facing channels through secure offerings, classified-environment access, accredited cloud stacks, and even military personnel embedding rather than engaging the state only through lobbying or abstract policy rhetoric.
- [Appendix B70](#ch-08-appendix-b70-frontier-legitimacy-is-increasingly-stabilized-through-a-combination-of-mission-r): That frontier legitimacy is increasingly stabilized through a combination of mission rhetoric, elite board composition, security credentials, policy access, and political spending.
- [Appendix B71](#ch-08-appendix-b71-the-legitimacy-field-around-frontier-ai-is-now-reinforced-not-only-by-boards-pol): That the legitimacy field around frontier AI is now reinforced not only by boards, policy essays, and lobbying, but by operational state interfaces that normalize privileged access and public-private dependence.
- [Appendix B72](#ch-08-appendix-b72-the-strongest-bounded-reading-of-elite-fallback-architecture-is-a-revealed-prefer): That the strongest bounded reading of elite fallback architecture is a revealed-preference gap between public stewardship language and private survivability hedging.
- [Appendix B73](#ch-08-appendix-b73-openai-s-governance-rupture-safety-rhetoric-leadership-churn-and-reasoning-mod): That OpenAI's governance rupture, safety rhetoric, leadership churn, and reasoning-model rollout are best read as parts of one reorganization of authority rather than as isolated episodes.
- [Appendix B74](#ch-08-appendix-b74-named-individual-disputes-should-enter-the-dossier-only-when-they-clarify-governa): That named-individual disputes should enter the dossier only when they clarify governance structure, institutional control, or legal architecture rather than functioning as scandal collage.

### Appendix E3. Speculative Narrative Risks {#ch-08-appendix-e3-speculative-narrative-risks}

- [Appendix B75](#ch-08-appendix-b75-bunker-ownership-survival-planning-or-retreat-architecture-prove-elite-foreknow): That bunker ownership, survival planning, or retreat architecture prove elite foreknowledge of imminent collapse or that frontier firms are knowingly engineering it.
- [Appendix B76](#ch-08-appendix-b76-elite-overlap-among-frontier-labs-venture-capital-lobbying-networks-and-defens): That elite overlap among frontier labs, venture capital, lobbying networks, and defense-adjacent institutions proves a single hidden command chain.
- [Appendix B77](#ch-08-appendix-b77-founder-philanthropy-or-public-warnings-about-concentration-prove-elite-foreknowl): That founder philanthropy or public warnings about concentration prove elite foreknowledge of imminent collapse or absolve frontier firms of responsibility.
- [Appendix B78](#ch-08-appendix-b78-formal-government-only-model-channels-classified-distribution-stacks-or-militar): That formal government-only model channels, classified distribution stacks, or military personnel embedding by themselves prove unlawful capture or total hidden state control over frontier AI firms.
- [Appendix B79](#ch-08-appendix-b79-elite-overlap-litigation-conflict-or-reputational-controversy-around-named-indi): That elite overlap, litigation conflict, or reputational controversy around named individuals justifies guilt by association without direct structural proof.

### Appendix E4. Sensitive or Review-Worthy Note Flags {#ch-08-appendix-e4-sensitive-or-review-worthy-note-flags}

- [Appendix A8](#ch-08-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers). Companion note: Note N-068. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That by June 6, 2025 Anthropic had publicly launched a government-specific model line for U.S. national security customers.
- [Appendix A10](#ch-08-appendix-a10-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation). Companion note: Note N-069. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That by June 13, 2025 the U.S. Army had created a formal structure to embed senior commercial-tech executives in a military innovation role.
- [Appendix A7](#ch-08-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government). Companion note: Note N-072. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That by June 16, 2025 OpenAI had formally reorganized and expanded its U.S. public-sector work under an `OpenAI for Government` initiative rather than treating government use as incidental.
- [Appendix A20](#ch-08-appendix-a20-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan). Companion note: Note N-073. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That OpenAI officially defended a public-policy agenda combining global commercial expansion, export controls, and preservation of access to copyrighted material for training.
- [Appendix A9](#ch-08-appendix-a9-anthropic-and-palantir-partner-to-bring-claude-ai-models-to-aws-for-u-s-government-intelligence-and-defense-operations). Companion note: Note N-074. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That by November 7, 2024 Anthropic model access for U.S. intelligence and defense agencies was being publicly distributed through a Palantir-plus-AWS stack.
- [Appendix A2](#ch-08-appendix-a2-openai-appoints-former-top-us-cyberwarrior-paul-nakasone-to-its-board-of-directors). Companion note: Note N-079. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That OpenAI brought a former top U.S. military and intelligence official directly into its governance structure.
- [Appendix A3](#ch-08-appendix-a3-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter). Companion note: Note N-081. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That frontier AI firms were spending meaningful and rising sums on federal lobbying by early 2026.
- [Appendix A12](#ch-08-appendix-a12-the-adolescence-of-technology). Companion note: Note N-084. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That by January 1, 2026, Anthropic's own CEO publicly described wealth concentration, backlash, and democratic erosion as serious AI-adjacent risks.
- [Appendix A11](#ch-08-appendix-a11-leading-the-future-committee-overview). Companion note: Note N-085. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That there is an official federal record for `LEADING THE FUTURE` as an active Super PAC in the 2025-2026 election cycle.
- [Appendix A6](#ch-08-appendix-a6-openai-appoints-one-of-wall-street-s-most-powerful-dealmakers-to-its-board). Companion note: Note N-087. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That OpenAI continued to load its board with elite finance and infrastructure figures during its restructuring phase.
- [Appendix A14](#ch-08-appendix-a14-sam-altman-s-manifest-destiny). Companion note: Note N-089. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That by October 3, 2016 Sam Altman publicly described maintaining a survival-prep cache and a New Zealand fallback with Peter Thiel.
- [Appendix A1](#ch-08-appendix-a1-openai-announces-leadership-transition). Companion note: Note N-091. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That OpenAI publicly treated its crisis as a governance and legitimacy rupture, not as a simple executive reshuffle.
- [Appendix A4](#ch-08-appendix-a4-openai-announces-new-members-to-board-of-directors). Companion note: Note N-092. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That OpenAI deliberately rebuilt its board with figures carrying regulatory, legal, nonprofit, and major-platform credentials.
- [Appendix A20](#ch-08-appendix-a20-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan). Companion note: Note N-093. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That OpenAI presents itself as a policy actor seeking to co-design national AI strategy.
- [Appendix A16](#ch-08-appendix-a16-mark-zuckerberg-downplays-his-5-000-square-foot-underground-bunker-in-hawaii-just-a-little-shelter). Companion note: Note N-094. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That Zuckerberg publicly minimized the compound's bunker framing after the earlier investigation.
- [Appendix A13](#ch-08-appendix-a13-encyclical-letter-of-his-holiness-leo-xiv-magnifica-humanitas). Companion note: Note N-095. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That by May 15, 2026, the AI legitimacy field had expanded into a formal Vatican encyclical rather than remaining confined to industry, policy, or technical discourse.
- [Appendix A15](#ch-08-appendix-a15-inside-mark-zuckerbergs-top-secret-hawaii-compound). Companion note: Note N-097. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That a serious `T2` investigation documented bunker-like features, strong secrecy controls, and private redundancy architecture at Zuckerberg's Kauai compound by December 2023.
- [Appendix A5](#ch-08-appendix-a5-openai-board-forms-safety-and-security-committee). Companion note: Note N-138. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That OpenAI publicly moved safety and security oversight to a named board-level structure after the governance crisis.
- [Appendix A1](#ch-08-appendix-a1-openai-announces-leadership-transition). Companion note: Note N-140. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That the November 2023 crisis was recognized by OpenAI itself as a formal governance rupture.
- [Appendix A18](#ch-08-appendix-a18-judge-denies-elon-musk-s-request-to-block-openai-for-profit-conversion-but-welcomes-trial). Companion note: Note N-144. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That by March 5, 2025 Musk had failed to obtain immediate judicial relief blocking OpenAI's conversion path.
- [Appendix A17](#ch-08-appendix-a17-musk-v-altman-4-24-cv-04722). Companion note: Note N-148. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That the Musk-OpenAI dispute had a concrete federal-court vehicle with named defendants and an active procedural history.
- [Appendix A19](#ch-08-appendix-a19-judge-denies-elon-musks-attempt-to-immediately-block-openais-conversion-to-for-profit-entity). Companion note: Note N-149. Risk flag: medium. Note focus: That by March 5, 2025 Musk had failed to obtain immediate judicial relief blocking OpenAI's conversion path.

# Chapter 09. Future if no action is taken

## Abstract

The most credible continuity scenario is not science-fiction apocalypse. The U.S. If no action is taken, platform immunity, private control over weights, and opaque state-company entanglement stop looking like separate policy issues and start looking like one reinforced system.

## Keywords

Future if no action is taken; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism; evidence-based dossier

## Research Question

How does future if no action is taken function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish?

## Scope and Framing Note

This paper examines future if no action is taken within the broader dossier *From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism*. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper that explains why inaction would compound dependency, hierarchy, and cognitive enclosure across the whole system. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 05, Chapter 07, Chapter 08, Chapter 10, but those cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic comprehension.

## Central Thesis

The most credible continuity scenario is not science-fiction apocalypse. It is a slower regime of deepening dependence, shrinking cognitive sovereignty, weakened middle-class bargaining power, and increasingly hierarchical access to machine intelligence. ^[Sources: U.S. Department of Education / ERIC, [Artificial Intelligence and the Futur...](https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097) (2023-05-24; see [Appendix A1](#ch-09-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations)); arXiv, [GPTs are GPTs: An Early Look at the L...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.10130) (2023-03-17; see [Appendix A2](#ch-09-appendix-a2-gpts-are-gpts-an-early-look-at-the-labor-market-impact-potential-of-large-language-models)); arXiv, [Computing Power and the Governance of...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08797) (2024-02-13; see [Appendix A3](#ch-09-appendix-a3-computing-power-and-the-governance-of-artificial-intelligence))]

## Evidence and Method Note

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 09 and is grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

## Introduction

This paper asks: How does future if no action is taken function within the dossier's larger argument, and what does the documentary record allow this chapter to establish? Its working answer is clear from the start: The most credible continuity scenario is not science-fiction apocalypse. It is a slower regime of deepening dependence, shrinking cognitive sovereignty, weakened middle-class bargaining power, and increasingly hierarchical access to machine intelligence. ^[Sources: U.S. Department of Education / ERIC, [Artificial Intelligence and the Futur...](https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097) (2023-05-24; see [Appendix A1](#ch-09-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations)); arXiv, [GPTs are GPTs: An Early Look at the L...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.10130) (2023-03-17; see [Appendix A2](#ch-09-appendix-a2-gpts-are-gpts-an-early-look-at-the-labor-market-impact-potential-of-large-language-models)); arXiv, [Computing Power and the Governance of...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08797) (2024-02-13; see [Appendix A3](#ch-09-appendix-a3-computing-power-and-the-governance-of-artificial-intelligence))] The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

## Historical or Institutional Context

The U.S. Department of Education has already framed AI in learning environments as a governance issue involving automated decisions, bias, privacy, and human oversight. Dependency concerns are therefore not merely cultural panic. ^[Sources: U.S. Department of Education / ERIC, [Artificial Intelligence and the Futur...](https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097) (2023-05-24; see [Appendix A1](#ch-09-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations))]

## Main Analysis

The GPTs paper and the IMF note both support an uneven future in which exposure to generative AI is distributed asymmetrically across occupations, income positions, and labor systems. The future problem is not uniform extinction. It is differential vulnerability. ^[Sources: arXiv, [GPTs are GPTs: An Early Look at the L...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.10130) (2023-03-17; see [Appendix A2](#ch-09-appendix-a2-gpts-are-gpts-an-early-look-at-the-labor-market-impact-potential-of-large-language-models)); IMF, [Gen-AI: Artificial Intelligence and t...](https://www.imf.org/en/publications/staff-discussion-notes/issues/2024/01/14/gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work-542379) (2024-01-14; see [Appendix A4](#ch-09-appendix-a4-gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work))]

Compute-governance research supports the claim that high-end capability is likely to remain infrastructure-concentrated unless policy changes the terms of access. That means unequal access to machine intelligence is already an infrastructure question, not only a talent question. ^[Sources: arXiv, [Computing Power and the Governance of...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08797) (2024-02-13; see [Appendix A3](#ch-09-appendix-a3-computing-power-and-the-governance-of-artificial-intelligence))]

The continuity scenario also includes unequal fallback. Some of the same actors asking institutions and publics to normalize deeper dependence on AI and platform infrastructure have also publicly described or been credibly reported as building private redundancy, jurisdictional exit, and survivability options: Altman's 2016 prep disclosures and New Zealand fallback, Zuckerberg's Kauai shelter complex, and Thiel's long association with New Zealand retreat logic and elite-exit politics. That does not prove foreknowledge, coordinated collapse engineering, or a hidden command chain. It does show how continuity can stratify not only access to intelligence, but access to backup, redundancy, and escape when shared systems fail or become politically untrustworthy. ^[Sources: Tad Friend, [Sam Altman's Manifest Destiny](https://www.newyorker.com/magazine/2016/10/10/sam-altmans-manifest-destiny) (2016-10-03; see [Appendix A5](#ch-09-appendix-a5-sam-altman-s-manifest-destiny)); Makena Kelly, [Inside Mark Zuckerberg’s Top-Secret H...](https://www.wired.com/story/mark-zuckerberg-inside-hawaii-compound) (2023-12-14; see [Appendix A6](#ch-09-appendix-a6-inside-mark-zuckerbergs-top-secret-hawaii-compound)); Charlotte Phillipp, [Mark Zuckerberg Downplays His 5,000-S...](https://people.com/mark-zuckerberg-hawaii-bunker-5000-square-feet-8765376) (2024-12-20; see [Appendix A7](#ch-09-appendix-a7-mark-zuckerberg-downplays-his-5-000-square-foot-underground-bunker-in-hawaii-just-a-little-shelter)); Mark O'Connell, [Why Silicon Valley billionaires are p...](https://www.theguardian.com/news/2018/feb/15/why-silicon-valley-billionaires-are-prepping-for-the-apocalypse-in-new-zealand) (2018-02-15; see [Appendix A8](#ch-09-appendix-a8-why-silicon-valley-billionaires-are-prepping-for-the-apocalypse-in-new-zealand)); Rebecca Rose, [New Zealand woos the wealthy with ‘go...](https://www.ft.com/content/76ca455c-3b16-4413-8738-3f17431d125b) (2025-09-24; see [Appendix A9](#ch-09-appendix-a9-new-zealand-woos-the-wealthy-with-golden-visa-changes))]

The financial and moral-warning layers also matter. The BIS warned of fragile capex dynamics and weak near-term returns in the AI buildout, while Dario Amodei publicly warned that AI-era wealth concentration could strain democratic society. These are not identical claims, but together they strengthen the dossier's continuity scenario of hierarchy rather than broad emancipation. ^[Sources: Bank for International Settlements, [Annual Economic Report 2026](https://www.bis.org/publ/arpdf/ar2026e.pdf) (2026-06-28; see [Appendix A10](#ch-09-appendix-a10-annual-economic-report-2026)); Dario Amodei, [The Adolescence of Technology](https://darioamodei.com/essay/the-adolescence-of-technology) (2026-01-01; see [Appendix A11](#ch-09-appendix-a11-the-adolescence-of-technology))]

Interpretation: if current platform habits, labor structures, weights enclosure, compute chokepoints, and asymmetric fallback systems continue reinforcing one another, the likely future is not total machine takeover but a regime of dependency, access hierarchy, and weaker cognitive sovereignty. A contextual formulation from Dion Wiggins is useful here: continuity can disguise dependency when systems still appear to function while effective authority migrates elsewhere. That is precisely why the dossier's three reforms belong together rather than as isolated policy tweaks. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A12](#ch-09-appendix-a12-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); Meta, [Mark Zuckerberg's Message to Meta Emp...](https://about.fb.com/news/2023/03/mark-zuckerberg-meta-year-of-efficiency/) (2023-03-14; see [Appendix A13](#ch-09-appendix-a13-mark-zuckerberg-s-message-to-meta-employees)); arXiv, [Computing Power and the Governance of...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08797) (2024-02-13; see [Appendix A3](#ch-09-appendix-a3-computing-power-and-the-governance-of-artificial-intelligence)); Dario Amodei, [The Adolescence of Technology](https://darioamodei.com/essay/the-adolescence-of-technology) (2026-01-01; see [Appendix A11](#ch-09-appendix-a11-the-adolescence-of-technology)); Dion Wiggins, [The Digital Sovereignty Imperative](https://digitalsovereigntyimperative.com/) (n.d.; see [Appendix A14](#ch-09-appendix-a14-the-digital-sovereignty-imperative))]

## Position Within the Series

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest companions are Chapter 05 (Talent, overhiring, valuation, and layoffs), Chapter 07 (Imperial control over access), Chapter 08 (Power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture), Chapter 10 (What to do). In that series logic, Chapter 09 explains why inaction would compound dependency, hierarchy, and cognitive enclosure across the whole system.

## Counterarguments

This chapter must remain explicit that it is a projection built from documented trend lines, not a prophecy. The evidence is stronger on labor exposure, governance risk, and access concentration than on long-run learning outcomes or a single inevitable end state. ^[Sources: U.S. Department of Education / ERIC, [Artificial Intelligence and the Futur...](https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097) (2023-05-24; see [Appendix A1](#ch-09-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations)); IMF, [Gen-AI: Artificial Intelligence and t...](https://www.imf.org/en/publications/staff-discussion-notes/issues/2024/01/14/gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work-542379) (2024-01-14; see [Appendix A4](#ch-09-appendix-a4-gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work))]

## Limits of the Evidence

This chapter must remain explicit that it is a projection built from documented trend lines, not a prophecy. The evidence is stronger on labor exposure, governance risk, and access concentration than on long-run learning outcomes or a single inevitable end state. ^[Sources: U.S. Department of Education / ERIC, [Artificial Intelligence and the Futur...](https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097) (2023-05-24; see [Appendix A1](#ch-09-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations)); IMF, [Gen-AI: Artificial Intelligence and t...](https://www.imf.org/en/publications/staff-discussion-notes/issues/2024/01/14/gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work-542379) (2024-01-14; see [Appendix A4](#ch-09-appendix-a4-gen-ai-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-work))]

## Reform Relevance

If no action is taken, platform immunity, private control over weights, and opaque state-company entanglement stop looking like separate policy issues and start looking like one reinforced system. That is why `Refactor Section 230`, `AI Weights as Patrimony of Humanity`, and `Limit Government Capture by AI Companies` must be read as one program of democratic reassertion. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A12](#ch-09-appendix-a12-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); OpenAI, [OpenAI’s proposals for the U.S. AI Ac...](https://openai.com/global-affairs/openai-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan/) (2025-03-13; see [Appendix A15](#ch-09-appendix-a15-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan)); arXiv, [Computing Power and the Governance of...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08797) (2024-02-13; see [Appendix A3](#ch-09-appendix-a3-computing-power-and-the-governance-of-artificial-intelligence))]

## Conclusion

If no action is taken, platform immunity, private control over weights, and opaque state-company entanglement stop looking like separate policy issues and start looking like one reinforced system. The final chapter therefore has to answer the question the whole dossier implies: what does resistance look like once the solution space is no longer open-ended but doctrinally explicit? In the architecture of this series, Chapter 09 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

## Notes

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among `Documented Fact`, `Disputed Fact`, `Hypothesis / Interpretation`, and `Speculative Narrative Risk`. In Chapter 09, those boundaries remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix sections that summarize claims and caution points.

# Chapter 10. What to do

## Abstract

The response program is no longer an open-ended menu. The platform chapters already established that sovereign-scale systems shape exposure, amplification, targeting, monetization, and moderation conditions at mass scale. The essential political choice is whether intelligence infrastructure will remain a privately concentrated empire built on captured social life, or whether societies will recover sovereignty over the terms on which cognition itself is organized.

## Keywords

What to do; surveillance capitalism; AI imperialism; Section 230 reform; AI weights; patrimony of humanity; government capture; evidence-based dossier

## Research Question

What reform doctrine follows from the dossier's evidence on platform power, frontier-model enclosure, and state-company entanglement?

## Scope and Framing Note

This paper examines what to do within the broader dossier *From Surveillance Capitalism to AI Imperialism*. It is intentionally written to circulate on its own: necessary context is restated, evidence boundaries are made explicit, and cited material is reproduced in paper-local form rather than delegated to repo navigation. It contributes to the series as a paper that synthesizes the entire dossier into the direct three-reform program. Adjacent reinforcement appears in Chapter 00, Chapter 01, Chapter 02, Chapter 04, Chapter 07, Chapter 08, Chapter 09, but those cross-references are supportive rather than required for basic comprehension.

## Central Thesis

The response program is no longer an open-ended menu. The dossier now takes a direct three-part stance:

- `Refactor Section 230`
- `AI Weights as Patrimony of Humanity`
- `Limit Government Capture by AI Companies`

Cognitive literacy, digital hygiene, labor organization, competition policy, and public-interest infrastructure remain necessary, but they function as implementation arms of these three reforms rather than as disconnected alternatives. ^[Sources: U.S. Department of Education / ERIC, [Artificial Intelligence and the Futur...](https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097) (2023-05-24; see [Appendix A1](#ch-10-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations)); arXiv, [In Oxford Handbook on AI Governance:...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.07700) (2021-08-05; see [Appendix A2](#ch-10-appendix-a2-in-oxford-handbook-on-ai-governance-the-role-of-workers-in-ai-ethics-and-governance)); arXiv, [Interactions Between Artificial Intel...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05761) (2024-12-07; see [Appendix A3](#ch-10-appendix-a3-interactions-between-artificial-intelligence-and-digital-public-infrastructure-concepts-benefits-and-challenges))]

## Evidence and Method Note

This paper is derived from the chapter corpus for Chapter 10 and is grounded in the project's structured research OS. It relies on source notes, extracted claims, entity profiles, and timeline events already normalized under the dossier's evidence hierarchy. Documented facts are asserted directly where the record supports them; disputed matters are marked as such; interpretive claims are bounded explicitly; and speculative overreach is isolated in the evidence-boundary appendix.

## Introduction

This paper asks: What reform doctrine follows from the dossier's evidence on platform power, frontier-model enclosure, and state-company entanglement? Its working answer is clear from the start: The response program is no longer an open-ended menu. The dossier now takes a direct three-part stance:

- `Refactor Section 230`
- `AI Weights as Patrimony of Humanity`
- `Limit Government Capture by AI Companies`

Cognitive literacy, digital hygiene, labor organization, competition policy, and public-interest infrastructure remain necessary, but they function as implementation arms of these three reforms rather than as disconnected alternatives. ^[Sources: U.S. Department of Education / ERIC, [Artificial Intelligence and the Futur...](https://eric.ed.gov/?id=ED631097) (2023-05-24; see [Appendix A1](#ch-10-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations)); arXiv, [In Oxford Handbook on AI Governance:...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.07700) (2021-08-05; see [Appendix A2](#ch-10-appendix-a2-in-oxford-handbook-on-ai-governance-the-role-of-workers-in-ai-ethics-and-governance)); arXiv, [Interactions Between Artificial Intel...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05761) (2024-12-07; see [Appendix A3](#ch-10-appendix-a3-interactions-between-artificial-intelligence-and-digital-public-infrastructure-concepts-benefits-and-challenges))] The objective is not only to recount developments, but to show why the subject of this chapter belongs inside a structural argument about extraction, enclosure, legitimacy, and reform.

## Historical or Institutional Context

The institutional context for what to do cannot be treated as an isolated debate. Each chapter in this series is part of a longer sequence in which platform design, data capture, labor correction, model governance, and state access accumulate into a single political structure. This section therefore identifies the historical or institutional setting needed for a standalone reader before moving deeper into the paper's own evidentiary claims.

## Main Analysis

The platform chapters already established that sovereign-scale systems shape exposure, amplification, targeting, monetization, and moderation conditions at mass scale. The training chapters established that frontier capability is built from collectively produced expression under contested legal conditions. The access and legitimacy chapters established that frontier firms already move through policy formation, strategic access regimes, lobbying, procurement channels, and public-private legitimacy structures. This final chapter turns those upstream findings into doctrine. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A4](#ch-10-appendix-a4-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); HHS, [Social Media and Youth Mental Health:...](https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html) (n.d.; see [Appendix A5](#ch-10-appendix-a5-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory)); U.S. Copyright Office, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf) (2025-05-09; see [Appendix A6](#ch-10-appendix-a6-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version)); OpenAI, [Introducing OpenAI for Government](https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/) (2025-06-16; see [Appendix A7](#ch-10-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government)); Anthropic, [Claude Gov models for U.S. national s...](https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers) (2025-06-06; see [Appendix A8](#ch-10-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers)); U.S. Army Public Affairs, [Army Launches Detachment 201: Executi...](https://www.army.mil/article/286317/army_launches_detachment_201_executive_innovation_corps_to_drive_tech_transformation) (2025-06-13; see [Appendix A9](#ch-10-appendix-a9-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation))]

### Refactor Section 230

Documented problem: sovereign-scale platforms do not merely host speech. They structure exposure, optimize amplification, monetize targeting, and govern the conditions under which civic, psychological, and political harm scales. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A4](#ch-10-appendix-a4-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); HHS, [Social Media and Youth Mental Health:...](https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html) (n.d.; see [Appendix A5](#ch-10-appendix-a5-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory)); arXiv, [When Kids Mode Isn't For Kids: Invest...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00299) (2025-06-30; see [Appendix A10](#ch-10-appendix-a10-when-kids-mode-isn-t-for-kids-investigating-tiktok-s-under-13-experience)); Pew Research Center, [Social Media Seen as Mostly Good for...](https://www.pewresearch.org/global/2022/12/06/social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier/) (2022-12-06; see [Appendix A11](#ch-10-appendix-a11-social-media-seen-as-mostly-good-for-democracy-across-many-nations-but-u-s-is-a-major-outlier))]

Interpretation: the legal principle should shift from passive hosting to systemic design responsibility. Refactoring Section `230` does not require treating every user post as if it were authored by the platform. It requires treating sovereign-scale platforms as infrastructures whose reach, design, and monetized amplification create duties commensurate with their power. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A4](#ch-10-appendix-a4-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); HHS, [Social Media and Youth Mental Health:...](https://www.hhs.gov/surgeongeneral/priorities/youth-mental-health/social-media/index.html) (n.d.; see [Appendix A5](#ch-10-appendix-a5-social-media-and-youth-mental-health-the-u-s-surgeon-general-s-advisory)); arXiv, [Quantifying social organization and p...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.00590) (2020-10-01; see [Appendix A12](#ch-10-appendix-a12-quantifying-social-organization-and-political-polarization-in-online-platforms))]

Policy direction: obligations should track reach, recommendation architecture, amplification, targeting, monetization, moderation architecture, and measurable systemic harm. Child-safety design duties, recommendation auditability, and sovereign-scale platform-risk obligations fit this framework more naturally than either the old passive-intermediary fiction or blanket publisher liability. ^[Sources: arXiv, [When Kids Mode Isn't For Kids: Invest...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00299) (2025-06-30; see [Appendix A10](#ch-10-appendix-a10-when-kids-mode-isn-t-for-kids-investigating-tiktok-s-under-13-experience)); arXiv, [Unfiltered: How Teens Engage in Body...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02176) (2025-04-02; see [Appendix A13](#ch-10-appendix-a13-unfiltered-how-teens-engage-in-body-image-and-shaming-discussions-via-instagram-direct-messages-dms)); FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A4](#ch-10-appendix-a4-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services))]

### AI Weights as Patrimony of Humanity

Documented problem: frontier-model capability is built from humanity's accumulated language, art, code, labor, science, and socially filtered judgment under legal conditions that remain actively contested. ^[Sources: U.S. Copyright Office, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf) (2025-05-09; see [Appendix A6](#ch-10-appendix-a6-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version)); GovInfo, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2023-08-30/pdf/2023-18624.pdf) (2023-08-30; see [Appendix A14](#ch-10-appendix-a14-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-notice-of-inquiry)); OpenAI, [Language Models are Unsupervised Mult...](https://cdn.openai.com/better-language-models/language_models_are_unsupervised_multitask_learners.pdf) (2019-02-14; see [Appendix A15](#ch-10-appendix-a15-language-models-are-unsupervised-multitask-learners)); AP, [Reddit strikes $60M deal allowing Goo...](https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708) (2024-02-22; see [Appendix A16](#ch-10-appendix-a16-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans))]

Interpretation: once that structure is clear, frontier weights cannot be treated as ordinary private property by default. The dossier's strongest normative direction is patrimony-oriented governance: public-trust logic, anti-monopoly limits, democratic oversight, interoperability duties, and restrictions on exclusive commercialization. In its strongest form, this doctrine pushes toward AI treated more like civic utility infrastructure: open and as broadly accessible as public-interest governance, safety constraints, and durable shared infrastructure can make it. This is advocated doctrine, not settled law. ^[Sources: TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A17](#ch-10-appendix-a17-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law)); U.S. House of Representatives, [Bipartisan House Task Force Report on...](https://www.speaker.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/AI-Task-Force-Report-FINAL.pdf) (2024-12-17; see [Appendix A18](#ch-10-appendix-a18-bipartisan-house-task-force-report-on-artificial-intelligence)); U.S. Copyright Office, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf) (2025-05-09; see [Appendix A6](#ch-10-appendix-a6-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version)); U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California via CourtListener, [Bartz et al. v. Anthropic PBC, Order...](https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.cand.434709/gov.uscourts.cand.434709.231.0_3.pdf) (2025-06-23; see [Appendix A19](#ch-10-appendix-a19-bartz-et-al-v-anthropic-pbc-order-on-fair-use))]

Policy direction: transparency around material training inputs, anti-enclosure limits for dominant model holders, democratic governance mechanisms, and restrictions on exclusive commercialization where public support is material belong inside this reform. ^[Sources: U.S. Copyright Office, [Copyright and Artificial Intelligence...](https://copyright.gov/ai/Copyright-and-Artificial-Intelligence-Part-3-Generative-AI-Training-Report-Pre-Publication-Version.pdf) (2025-05-09; see [Appendix A6](#ch-10-appendix-a6-copyright-and-artificial-intelligence-part-3-generative-ai-training-pre-publication-version)); OpenAI, [OpenAI’s proposals for the U.S. AI Ac...](https://openai.com/global-affairs/openai-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan/) (2025-03-13; see [Appendix A20](#ch-10-appendix-a20-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan)); AP, [Reddit strikes $60M deal allowing Goo...](https://apnews.com/article/a7f131c7cb4225307134ef21d3c6a708) (2024-02-22; see [Appendix A16](#ch-10-appendix-a16-reddit-strikes-60m-deal-allowing-google-to-train-ai-models-on-its-posts-unveils-ipo-plans))]

### Limit Government Capture by AI Companies

Documented problem: frontier firms already participate in policy formation, export-control protection, government-only offerings, classified-environment distribution channels, lobbying, electoral vehicles, and military-adjacent personnel structures. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [OpenAI’s proposals for the U.S. AI Ac...](https://openai.com/global-affairs/openai-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan/) (2025-03-13; see [Appendix A20](#ch-10-appendix-a20-openais-proposals-for-the-us-ai-action-plan)); OpenAI, [Introducing OpenAI for Government](https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/) (2025-06-16; see [Appendix A7](#ch-10-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government)); Anthropic, [Claude Gov models for U.S. national s...](https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers) (2025-06-06; see [Appendix A8](#ch-10-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers)); Palantir Technologies, [Anthropic and Palantir Partner to Bri...](https://investors.palantir.com/news-details/2024/Anthropic-and-Palantir-Partner-to-Bring-Claude-AI-Models-to-AWS-for-U.S.-Government-Intelligence-and-Defense-Operations/) (2024-11-07; see [Appendix A21](#ch-10-appendix-a21-anthropic-and-palantir-partner-to-bring-claude-ai-models-to-aws-for-u-s-government-intelligence-and-defense-operations)); U.S. Army Public Affairs, [Army Launches Detachment 201: Executi...](https://www.army.mil/article/286317/army_launches_detachment_201_executive_innovation_corps_to_drive_tech_transformation) (2025-06-13; see [Appendix A9](#ch-10-appendix-a9-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation)); Axios, [Anthropic outspends OpenAI in biggest...](https://www.axios.com/2026/04/21/anthropic-outspends-openai-biggest-lobbying-quarter) (2026-04-21; see [Appendix A22](#ch-10-appendix-a22-anthropic-outspends-openai-in-biggest-ever-lobbying-quarter))]

Interpretation: any frontier firm benefiting materially from public funding, procurement, compute support, export-control protection, regulatory protection, or national-security access should face heightened public obligations. Public support should increase transparency duties rather than deepen private enclosure. This is an anti-capture doctrine, not a generic appeal to ethics and not a claim of total hidden control. ^[Sources: OpenAI, [Introducing OpenAI for Government](https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/) (2025-06-16; see [Appendix A7](#ch-10-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government)); Anthropic, [Claude Gov models for U.S. national s...](https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers) (2025-06-06; see [Appendix A8](#ch-10-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers)); Federal Election Commission, [LEADING THE FUTURE - committee overview](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00916114/) (2026-03-31; see [Appendix A23](#ch-10-appendix-a23-leading-the-future-committee-overview)); Dario Amodei, [The Adolescence of Technology](https://darioamodei.com/essay/the-adolescence-of-technology) (2026-01-01; see [Appendix A24](#ch-10-appendix-a24-the-adolescence-of-technology))]

Policy direction: procurement visibility, subsidy transparency, lobbying disclosure, conflict-of-interest rules, revolving-door limits, independent audits, model-risk reporting, and limits on exclusive private benefit from publicly supported AI infrastructure should be the baseline. Where firms are simultaneously building quasi-public intelligence infrastructure and private survivability hedges, the burden for public accountability should rise further: dependency-building should not be paired with private exemption from shared failure without stronger oversight and obligations. ^[Sources: AP, [FTC opens inquiry into Big Tech's par...](https://apnews.com/article/67feef411ef311f0be543f546ef34b3d) (2024-01-25; see [Appendix A25](#ch-10-appendix-a25-ftc-opens-inquiry-into-big-tech-s-partnerships-with-leading-ai-startups)); OpenAI, [Introducing OpenAI for Government](https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/) (2025-06-16; see [Appendix A7](#ch-10-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government)); Anthropic, [Claude Gov models for U.S. national s...](https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers) (2025-06-06; see [Appendix A8](#ch-10-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers)); U.S. Army Public Affairs, [Army Launches Detachment 201: Executi...](https://www.army.mil/article/286317/army_launches_detachment_201_executive_innovation_corps_to_drive_tech_transformation) (2025-06-13; see [Appendix A9](#ch-10-appendix-a9-army-launches-detachment-201-executive-innovation-corps-to-drive-tech-transformation)); Tad Friend, [Sam Altman's Manifest Destiny](https://www.newyorker.com/magazine/2016/10/10/sam-altmans-manifest-destiny) (2016-10-03; see [Appendix A26](#ch-10-appendix-a26-sam-altman-s-manifest-destiny)); Makena Kelly, [Inside Mark Zuckerberg’s Top-Secret H...](https://www.wired.com/story/mark-zuckerberg-inside-hawaii-compound) (2023-12-14; see [Appendix A27](#ch-10-appendix-a27-inside-mark-zuckerbergs-top-secret-hawaii-compound)); Rebecca Rose, [New Zealand woos the wealthy with ‘go...](https://www.ft.com/content/76ca455c-3b16-4413-8738-3f17431d125b) (2025-09-24; see [Appendix A28](#ch-10-appendix-a28-new-zealand-woos-the-wealthy-with-golden-visa-changes))]

## Position Within the Series

This paper sits inside a coordinated 11-paper architecture. Its nearest companions are Chapter 00 (Opening framework), Chapter 01 (Social networks as infrastructure for free data capture), Chapter 02 (Social erosion and moral reconfiguration), Chapter 04 (AI arrives when the human raw material already exists), Chapter 07 (Imperial control over access), Chapter 08 (Power network, legitimacy, and institutional capture), Chapter 09 (Future if no action is taken). In that series logic, Chapter 10 synthesizes the entire dossier into the direct three-reform program.

## Counterarguments

This chapter is partly normative by design. The current corpus supports the legitimacy of a three-reform agenda, but not yet a fully implementation-ready statutory blueprint in every domain. That is not a weakness if the distinction remains explicit. ^[Sources: arXiv, [In Oxford Handbook on AI Governance:...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.07700) (2021-08-05; see [Appendix A2](#ch-10-appendix-a2-in-oxford-handbook-on-ai-governance-the-role-of-workers-in-ai-ethics-and-governance)); arXiv, [Interactions Between Artificial Intel...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05761) (2024-12-07; see [Appendix A3](#ch-10-appendix-a3-interactions-between-artificial-intelligence-and-digital-public-infrastructure-concepts-benefits-and-challenges)); AP, [FTC opens inquiry into Big Tech's par...](https://apnews.com/article/67feef411ef311f0be543f546ef34b3d) (2024-01-25; see [Appendix A25](#ch-10-appendix-a25-ftc-opens-inquiry-into-big-tech-s-partnerships-with-leading-ai-startups))]

## Limits of the Evidence

This chapter is partly normative by design. The current corpus supports the legitimacy of a three-reform agenda, but not yet a fully implementation-ready statutory blueprint in every domain. That is not a weakness if the distinction remains explicit. ^[Sources: arXiv, [In Oxford Handbook on AI Governance:...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.07700) (2021-08-05; see [Appendix A2](#ch-10-appendix-a2-in-oxford-handbook-on-ai-governance-the-role-of-workers-in-ai-ethics-and-governance)); arXiv, [Interactions Between Artificial Intel...](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05761) (2024-12-07; see [Appendix A3](#ch-10-appendix-a3-interactions-between-artificial-intelligence-and-digital-public-infrastructure-concepts-benefits-and-challenges)); AP, [FTC opens inquiry into Big Tech's par...](https://apnews.com/article/67feef411ef311f0be543f546ef34b3d) (2024-01-25; see [Appendix A25](#ch-10-appendix-a25-ftc-opens-inquiry-into-big-tech-s-partnerships-with-leading-ai-startups))]

## Reform Relevance

The essential political choice is whether intelligence infrastructure will remain a privately concentrated empire built on captured social life, or whether societies will recover sovereignty over the terms on which cognition itself is organized. The dossier's answer is explicit: sovereign-scale platform accountability, patrimony-oriented governance of frontier weights, and strong limits on opaque state-corporate capture. ^[Sources: FTC, [A Look Behind the Screens: Examining...](https://www.ftc.gov/reports/look-behind-screens-examining-data-practices-social-media-video-streaming-services) (n.d.; see [Appendix A4](#ch-10-appendix-a4-a-look-behind-the-screens-examining-the-data-practices-of-social-media-and-video-streaming-services)); TwinChat, [AI Copyright Weights: A New Frontier...](https://medium.com/twinchat/ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law-d8ee1b6c55ee) (n.d.; see [Appendix A17](#ch-10-appendix-a17-ai-copyright-weights-a-new-frontier-in-intellectual-property-law)); OpenAI, [Introducing OpenAI for Government](https://openai.com/global-affairs/introducing-openai-for-government/) (2025-06-16; see [Appendix A7](#ch-10-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government)); Anthropic, [Claude Gov models for U.S. national s...](https://www.anthropic.com/news/claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers) (2025-06-06; see [Appendix A8](#ch-10-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers))]

## Conclusion

The essential political choice is whether intelligence infrastructure will remain a privately concentrated empire built on captured social life, or whether societies will recover sovereignty over the terms on which cognition itself is organized. The dossier closes here. In the architecture of this series, Chapter 10 therefore functions as a self-contained argument while also advancing the cumulative path toward the dossier's three reforms.

## Notes

This paper is designed to circulate independently. Public notes point readers to the real external documents first, then to the paper-local appendix entry that explains why each source matters.

The underlying research OS distinguishes among `Documented Fact`, `Disputed Fact`, `Hypothesis / Interpretation`, and `Speculative Narrative Risk`. In Chapter 10, those boundaries remain visible in the prose, in the bibliography, and in the appendix sections that summarize claims and caution points.
