## Appendix E. Evidence Boundaries

### Appendix E1. Disputed Matters {#ch-10-appendix-e1-disputed-matters}

- [Appendix B38](#ch-10-appendix-b38-whether-existing-copyright-and-fair-use-doctrine-can-absorb-frontier-model-traini): That whether existing copyright and fair-use doctrine can absorb frontier-model training without consent or compensation remains actively contested across agencies, courts, and litigants.

### Appendix E2. Interpretive Boundaries {#ch-10-appendix-e2-interpretive-boundaries}

- [Appendix B39](#ch-10-appendix-b39-sovereign-scale-platforms-should-be-regulated-on-the-basis-of-design-amplificati): That sovereign-scale platforms should be regulated on the basis of design, amplification, targeting, monetization, moderation architecture, and systemic reach rather than passive-hosting logic.
- [Appendix B40](#ch-10-appendix-b40-documented-youth-exposure-recommendation-amplification-trust-erosion-and-fragm): That documented youth exposure, recommendation amplification, trust erosion, and fragmentation support a sovereign-scale duty framework under a refactored Section 230 focused on design, exposure, and systemic harm.
- [Appendix B41](#ch-10-appendix-b41-frontier-ai-weights-can-be-read-as-condensed-claims-over-collectively-produced-hu): That frontier AI weights can be read as condensed claims over collectively produced human expression and therefore justify patrimony-oriented governance.
- [Appendix B42](#ch-10-appendix-b42-current-official-records-show-frontier-labs-and-adjacent-platform-firms-building): That current official records show frontier labs and adjacent platform firms building formal government-facing channels through secure offerings, classified-environment access, accredited cloud stacks, and even military personnel embedding rather than engaging the state only through lobbying or abstract policy rhetoric.
- [Appendix B43](#ch-10-appendix-b43-firms-benefiting-from-export-control-protection-strategic-state-access-or-privi): That firms benefiting from export-control protection, strategic state access, or privileged public coordination should face stronger transparency and public-obligation duties than ordinary software vendors.
- [Appendix B44](#ch-10-appendix-b44-frontier-legitimacy-is-increasingly-stabilized-through-a-combination-of-mission-r): That frontier legitimacy is increasingly stabilized through a combination of mission rhetoric, elite board composition, security credentials, policy access, and political spending.
- [Appendix B45](#ch-10-appendix-b45-the-legitimacy-field-around-frontier-ai-is-now-reinforced-not-only-by-boards-pol): That the legitimacy field around frontier AI is now reinforced not only by boards, policy essays, and lobbying, but by operational state interfaces that normalize privileged access and public-private dependence.
- [Appendix B46](#ch-10-appendix-b46-the-strongest-bounded-reading-of-elite-fallback-architecture-is-a-revealed-prefer): That the strongest bounded reading of elite fallback architecture is a revealed-preference gap between public stewardship language and private survivability hedging.
- [Appendix B47](#ch-10-appendix-b47-the-continuity-scenario-includes-asymmetry-of-fallback-and-exit-with-elite-actor): That the continuity scenario includes asymmetry of fallback and exit, with elite actors purchasing redundancy while ordinary users are asked to normalize dependence.
- [Appendix B48](#ch-10-appendix-b48-if-current-platform-habits-labor-structures-and-compute-chokepoints-continue-re): That if current platform habits, labor structures, and compute chokepoints continue reinforcing one another, the most credible continuity scenario is dependency, access hierarchy, and weaker cognitive sovereignty.
- [Appendix B49](#ch-10-appendix-b49-frontier-firms-receiving-material-public-support-state-coordination-export-cont): That frontier firms receiving material public support, state coordination, export-control protection, or national-security access should face heightened transparency, audit, conflict-of-interest, procurement-visibility, and exclusive-benefit limits.
- [Appendix B50](#ch-10-appendix-b50-sovereign-scale-platforms-should-face-a-section-230-framework-keyed-to-systemic-r): That sovereign-scale platforms should face a Section 230 framework keyed to systemic reach, design, amplification, targeting, monetization, moderation architecture, and measurable harm rather than passive-hosting logic alone.
- [Appendix B51](#ch-10-appendix-b51-cognitive-literacy-labor-organization-competition-policy-and-public-interest-i): That cognitive literacy, labor organization, competition policy, and public-interest infrastructure should function as implementation arms of the dossier's three reforms rather than as disconnected options.
- [Appendix B52](#ch-10-appendix-b52-frontier-ai-weights-trained-on-collectively-produced-human-expression-justify-a-p): That frontier AI weights trained on collectively produced human expression justify a patrimony-oriented governance response including public-trust logic, anti-monopoly limits, democratic oversight, and restrictions on exclusive commercialization.

### Appendix E3. Speculative Narrative Risks {#ch-10-appendix-e3-speculative-narrative-risks}

- [Appendix B53](#ch-10-appendix-b53-refactoring-section-230-for-sovereign-scale-platforms-requires-treating-platforms): That refactoring Section 230 for sovereign-scale platforms requires treating platforms as ordinary publishers for every user utterance regardless of design, amplification, targeting, or monetization.
- [Appendix B54](#ch-10-appendix-b54-current-legal-uncertainty-or-congressional-uptake-already-proves-frontier-ai-weig): That current legal uncertainty or congressional uptake already proves frontier AI weights are legally settled as patrimony, public trust property, or common heritage.
- [Appendix B55](#ch-10-appendix-b55-bunker-ownership-survival-planning-or-retreat-architecture-prove-elite-foreknow): That bunker ownership, survival planning, or retreat architecture prove elite foreknowledge of imminent collapse or that frontier firms are knowingly engineering it.
- [Appendix B56](#ch-10-appendix-b56-founder-philanthropy-or-public-warnings-about-concentration-prove-elite-foreknowl): That founder philanthropy or public warnings about concentration prove elite foreknowledge of imminent collapse or absolve frontier firms of responsibility.
- [Appendix B57](#ch-10-appendix-b57-formal-government-only-model-channels-classified-distribution-stacks-or-militar): That formal government-only model channels, classified distribution stacks, or military personnel embedding by themselves prove unlawful capture or total hidden state control over frontier AI firms.
- [Appendix B58](#ch-10-appendix-b58-the-continuity-scenario-is-a-fixed-prophecy-of-inevitable-collapse-rather-than-a): That the continuity scenario is a fixed prophecy of inevitable collapse rather than a political trajectory that could still be altered by institutional reform.
- [Appendix B59](#ch-10-appendix-b59-individual-digital-hygiene-consumer-choice-or-personal-restraint-alone-can-solv): That individual digital hygiene, consumer choice, or personal restraint alone can solve sovereign-scale platform harm, enclosure of frontier weights, or opaque state-company entanglement without structural reform.
- [Appendix B60](#ch-10-appendix-b60-voluntary-elite-philanthropy-or-self-limitation-can-substitute-for-structural-ref): That voluntary elite philanthropy or self-limitation can substitute for structural reform of platform power, frontier-weight ownership, and state-company capture.

### Appendix E4. Sensitive or Review-Worthy Note Flags {#ch-10-appendix-e4-sensitive-or-review-worthy-note-flags}

- [Appendix A8](#ch-10-appendix-a8-claude-gov-models-for-u-s-national-security-customers). Companion note: Note N-068. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That by June 6, 2025 Anthropic had publicly launched a government-specific model line for U.S. national security customers.
- [Appendix A7](#ch-10-appendix-a7-introducing-openai-for-government). Companion note: Note N-072. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That by June 16, 2025 OpenAI had formally reorganized and expanded its U.S. public-sector work under an `OpenAI for Government` initiative rather than treating government use as incidental.
- [Appendix A21](#ch-10-appendix-a21-anthropic-and-palantir-partner-to-bring-claude-ai-models-to-aws-for-u-s-government-intelligence-and-defense-operations). Companion note: Note N-074. Risk flag: low. Note focus: That by November 7, 2024 Anthropic model access for U.S. intelligence and defense agencies was being publicly distributed through a Palantir-plus-AWS stack.
- [Appendix A24](#ch-10-appendix-a24-the-adolescence-of-technology). Companion note: Note N-084. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That by January 1, 2026, Anthropic's own CEO publicly described wealth concentration, backlash, and democratic erosion as serious AI-adjacent risks.
- [Appendix A26](#ch-10-appendix-a26-sam-altman-s-manifest-destiny). Companion note: Note N-089. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That by October 3, 2016 Sam Altman publicly described maintaining a survival-prep cache and a New Zealand fallback with Peter Thiel.
- [Appendix A30](#ch-10-appendix-a30-encyclical-letter-of-his-holiness-leo-xiv-magnifica-humanitas). Companion note: Note N-095. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That by May 15, 2026, the AI legitimacy field had expanded into a formal Vatican encyclical rather than remaining confined to industry, policy, or technical discourse.
- [Appendix A27](#ch-10-appendix-a27-inside-mark-zuckerbergs-top-secret-hawaii-compound). Companion note: Note N-097. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That a serious `T2` investigation documented bunker-like features, strong secrecy controls, and private redundancy architecture at Zuckerberg's Kauai compound by December 2023.
- [Appendix A3](#ch-10-appendix-a3-interactions-between-artificial-intelligence-and-digital-public-infrastructure-concepts-benefits-and-challenges). Companion note: Note N-103. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That a public-interest AI infrastructure layer is intellectually and technically legible within serious research, not an ideological fantasy.
- [Appendix A25](#ch-10-appendix-a25-ftc-opens-inquiry-into-big-tech-s-partnerships-with-leading-ai-startups). Companion note: Note N-104. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That formal competition scrutiny of frontier-AI partnerships was already underway in the United States by January 2024.
- [Appendix A1](#ch-10-appendix-a1-artificial-intelligence-and-the-future-of-teaching-and-learning-insights-and-recommendations). Companion note: Note N-107. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That an institutional response agenda can legitimately include cognitive literacy, human oversight, and training rather than relying on consumer self-control alone.
- [Appendix A2](#ch-10-appendix-a2-in-oxford-handbook-on-ai-governance-the-role-of-workers-in-ai-ethics-and-governance). Companion note: Note N-108. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That workers can be treated as governance actors in AI deployment rather than only as subjects of managerial adoption.
- [Appendix A28](#ch-10-appendix-a28-new-zealand-woos-the-wealthy-with-golden-visa-changes). Companion note: Note N-111. Risk flag: review. Note focus: That the Thiel/New Zealand strand can be grounded in a serious `T2` business-paper source, not only in contextual essays.
